Their paper, published by ArXiv in December 2023, reviews both the hardware and software techniques used and contains a frank discussion of the successes and failures, with a section about how to apply lessons learned to future expeditions. Both observable-light and infrared cameras are deployed, as well as sensors for other (non-EM) emissions. A pixel-subtraction method is augmented with other similarly simple methods to provide initial identification of objects in the sky and/or the sea crossing the cameras' fields of view. (Matthew Szydagis, et al., 'Initial Results from the First Field Expedition of UAPx to Study Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena,' arXiv, December 1, 2023)
- 2021, July 20 - Reports surface of American diplomatic officials coming down with Havana Syndrome in Kyrgyzstan and Uzbekistan. ('American Personnel in Kyrgyzstan, Uzbekistan Reported As among 'Havana Syndrome' Victims,' bne IntelliNews, July 21, 2021)
- 2021, July 21 - According to a Gallup poll, 41% of Americans think that at least some UFOs are alien spacecraft, up 8% from a similar question in 2019. Only 50% consider all sightings to be explainable, down 10% from 2019. (Lydia Saad, 'Do Americans Believe in UFOs?' Gallup News, August 20, 2021)
- 2021, July 26 - The Galileo Project, headed by a multi-institutional team of scientists led by Avi Loeb of the Department of Astronomy at Harvard University, announces that it will investigate evidence that could represent defunct or still-active 'extraterrestrial technological civilizations,' or ETCs. The project, which includes Luis Elizondo, Christopher Mellon, Nick Pope, and Michael Shermer, will analyze data from astronomical surveys and telescope observations, and design new algorithms using artificial intelligence, in order to identify potential interstellar travelers, alien-built satellites, and UAPs. Ufologist Jacques Vallée joins the project in January 2022. (Mindy Weisberger, 'Harvard-Led Team to Search Cosmos for Extraterrestrial Space Tech and UFOs,' Live Science, July 26, 2021; Robert Sheaffer, 'Galileos Galore: Now Including Dr. Jacques Vallée!' Bad UFOs, January 25, 2022)
- 2021, July 28 - A man in a jetpack is seen at an altitude 5,000 feet by pilots near Los Angeles International Airport. (John Greenewald, 'Jetpack Man Sighting over Los Angeles International Airport (LAX), July 28, 2021,' The Black Vault, May 2, 2023)
- 2021, July 30 - 11:00 p.m. A bright green fireball illuminates the night sky over Izmir, Turkey. The visual part of the event lasts several seconds before the object disintegrates. Several residents report a sonic boom. According to Hassan Ali Dar, deputy director of the Astronomical Observatory of Aegean University, the object is part of the Perseid meteor shower. (Teo Blašković, 'Very Bright Fireball over Izmir, Sonic Boom Reported, Turkey,' The Watchers, August 2, 2021)
- 2021, August - Reports of Havana Syndrome come to light among more than 20 US diplomats in Vienna, Austria, since January. The numbers are greater here than in any city outside Havana, Cuba. The CIA removes its station chief in Vienna for not adequately responding to the outbreak. (''Havana Syndrome'-Like Mystery Illness Affects Vienna US Diplomats,' BBC News, July 17, 2021; 'CIA 'Removes Vienna Boss' over Havana Syndrome Outbreak,' BBC News, September 24, 2021)
- 2021, August 29 - In a breakthrough interview on CBS-TV's 60 Minutes, US Navy pilots line up to recount their experiences with UFOs on the eastern coast. It happens so frequently that the encounters became commonplace, Ryan Graves, a retired navy pilot, tells the show. 'Every day,' Graves says. 'Every day for at least a couple years.' (Bill Whitaker, 'UFOs Regularly Spotted in Restricted US Airspace,' CBS News, August 29, 2021)
- 2021, September 5 - Australian journalist Ross Coulthart releases a documentary, The UFO Phenomenon, which recounts the history of the Australian government's involvement with UFOs. ('The UFO Phenomenon: Full Documentary 2021,' 7NEWS Spotlight YouTube channel, September 5, 2021)
- 2021, September 8 - A resident of Oxford, Connecticut, sees three bright but silent objects hovering for about 30-40 seconds right before an intense light appears that seems to be 'scanning from front to back.' Something or someone is silently lowered out of one of the lights into a field across the street, then quickly drawn back up. Shortly afterward, the lights flash and disappear, but the witness can still see light trails left behind. (Rich Kirby, 'Are UFOs over Connecticut Growing Bolder? (We May Never Know),' Patch Media, Brookfield, Conn., January 1, 2022)
- 2021, September 15 - 4:03 p.m. One adult and two children witness a hovering metallic object in the Century City area of Los Angeles, California. After watching it for a few seconds, the adult takes a video as the object slowly moves toward the east. ('Video Taken of Hovering Bright, Metallic, Flashing Object,' UFOs Northwest, October 12, 2021)
- 2021, September 28 - An F-35 pilot watches an unknown object while flying southwest at 32,000 feet above Gila Bend, Arizona. (Joseph Trevithick and Tyler Rogoway, 'Pilots Are Seeing Some Very Strange Things in Arizona's Military Training Ranges,' The Drive: The War Zone, August 2, 2023)
- 2021, October Skinwalkers at the Pentagon, by James T. Lacatski, Colm Kelleher, and George Knapp is published. It explains the nature and leadership of the Defense Intelligence Agency's Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications Program (AAWSAP), the amount of money it had at its disposal, and its scope, which encompassed both UFO performance and the effects of UFOs on humans. (Covert, xxviii-xxix)
- 2021, October - US diplomats in Bogota, Colombia, and Berlin, Germany, are being affected by Havana Syndrome. ('Havana Syndrome: Berlin Police Probe Cases at US Embassy,' BBC News, October 9, 2021; 'Havana Syndrome Reported at US Embassy in Colombia,' BBC News, October 13, 2021)
- 2021, October 8 - The Helping American Victims Afflicted by Neurological Attacks (HAVANA) Act is signed by President Joe Biden. It authorizes the CIA Director and the Secretary of State to provide financial support for personnel suffering brain injuries. However, while funding for it has not yet passed, it has been included in drafts of a Defense Department appropriations bill. ('Biden Signs Legislation to Compensate Victims of Mysterious 'Havana Syndrome,'' New York Times, October 8, 2021)
- 2021, October 9 - 1:23 p.m. Witnesses watch a small, rounded, silver-metallic object at high altitude (perhaps above 15,000 feet) for 5 minutes during the Wings Over Houston Air Show at Ellington Air Force Base near Houston, Texas. The object leaves no trail as it alternately maneuvers and hovers. One of them takes a photograph. The MUFON investigator suspects the object might be a US Army Long Endurance Multi Intelligence Vehicle, a hybrid blimp, making an appearance at the air show. (Mutual UFO Network, 'MUFON 118675'; National UFO Reporting Center, 'NUFORC UFO Sighting 164979' and 'NUFORC UFO Sighting 164980'; Kevin D. Randle, 'Coast to Coast: The National Defense Authorization Act and Two Interesting Sightings,' A Different Perspective, December 11, 2021)
- 2021, October 19 - The Department of Defense, Office of the Inspector General meets with Neill T. Tipton, director for defense intelligence, collection, and special programs, in regard to its actions regarding UAP. (John Greenewald,
- 'The Black Vault's AAWSAP/AATIP and Post 2017 UAP/UFO Timeline Project,' The Black Vault, January 21, 2023)
- 2021, October 25 - 8:30 a.m. A witness in Bootle, Merseyside, UK, takes a video that seems to show a comet-like UFO passing through the sky. Ufologist Jenny Randles identifies it as an Emirates Airbus 380 with the low sun illuminating its trail. (Jenny Randles, 'Liverpool Echoes,' Fortean Times 413 (Christmas 2021): 29)
- 2021, November - Optical engineer Vincent Costes takes over from Roger Baldacchino as manager of Groupe d'Études et d'Informations sur les Phénomènes Aérospatiaux Non-identifiés, the official French UFO agency in Toulouse, France. ('Un nouveau responsable au GEIPAN à partir de novembre 2021,' GEIPAN, November 3, 2021)
- 2021, November 4 - Sen. Kirsten Gillibrand (D-N.Y.) introduces an amendment to the National Defense Authorization Act that would establish an 'Anomaly Surveillance and Resolution Office' to replace the UAP Task Force in the Navy's Office of Naval Intelligence. (Bryan Bender, ''This Is Urgent': Bipartisan Proposal for UFO Office Pushes New Boundaries,' Politico, November 17, 2021)
- 2021, November 6 - 8.55 a.m. A witness in Marietta, Georgia, photographs a white, cigar-shaped object moving in a westerly direction. ('Strange Cigar-Shaped Object Seen and Photographed,' UFOs Northwest, November 23, 2021)
- 2021, November 8 - Night. A couple from Hubbard, Ohio, see a triangular-shaped object with a bright yellow light on top and two sets of three white lights along the side, just above the treetops. It appears to be football-field sized and is hovering about 100 feet above the highway. As they approach, it swiftly moves to the left and flies over some trees. Their car begins to act funny and the check-engine light comes on, indicating reduced engine power and traction control. (Kevin D. Randle, 'Coast to Coast AM: EM Effects and Current UFO Sightings,' A Different Perspective, December 17, 2021)
- 2021, November 11 - 5:13 a.m. A resident of Woodbury, Connecticut, sees a 'bright orange glowing object' that resembles an 'intense sunrise.' The fireball moves behind a hill and the sky returns to its normal nighttime color. (Rich Kirby, 'Are UFOs over Connecticut Growing Bolder? (We May Never Know),' Patch Media, Brookfield, Conn., January 1, 2022)
- 2021, November 11 - 11:10 a.m. A witness sees nine white orbs flying in formation and mimicking a flyby of F-16s during the Leavenworth, Kansas, Annual Veterans Day Parade. Prior to the F-16 flyby, the witness sees the orbs forming and reforming into various groups. At times they disappear and reappear at an incredible speed. One of the orbs is in an 'overwatch' position, while at least six are in a 'three-by-two formation.' After passing the flyby, they form a four-point formation then disappear to the west at great speed. A photo is taken showing the orbs and the F-16s. ('9 White Orbs Seen at F16 Airshow,' UFOs Northwest, November 19, 2021)
- 2021, November 19 - The governmental Centro de Identificatión Aerospaciale in Argentina issues an annual report on its investigation into 45 UFO cases analyzed during the previous year, plus occasional investigations on older events. CIAE investigates only those UFO sightings supported by evidence (photography, video, or material). All of them are technically explained, the distribution of causes being birds & bugs (40%), balloons and airborne objects (18%), optical artifacts (11%), astronomical (11%), astronautical (11%), aircraft (7%), or ground facilities (2%). (Rubén E. Lianza, 'Informe de Resolución de Casos Recibidos en 2021,' CIAE, November 19, 2021)
- 2021, November 22 - Morning. A joiner working on a wastewater plant in Dalmamock, East Glasgow, Scotland, sees two objects, one bright white and the other dark, hovering side by side and moving slightly up and down as a police helicopter arrives to investigate them. The witness takes clear video footage of the objects and the helicopter. After he stops filming, the objects climb vertically upward and disappear. Possible drones. (Jenny Randles, 'May Day! May Day!' Fortean Times 419 (June 2022): 27)
- 2021, November 23 - US Deputy Secretary of Defense Kathleen Hicks establishes the Airborne Object Identification and Management Synchronization Group (AOIMSG) to synchronize the detection and identification of UAPs. It replaces the Navy's UAP Task Force and is to be overseen and directed by the Airborne Object Identification and Management Executive Council (AOIMEXEC), led by Under Secretary of Defense for Intelligence and Security Ronald Moultrie. (Wikipedia, 'All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office'; Kathleen Hicks, 'Establishment of the Airborne Object Identification and Management Synchronization Group,' November 23, 2021; 'Ex-Officials Express Deep Concerns over New Pentagon UFO Unit,' The Hill, December 1, 2021; John Greenewald, 'DoD Refuses to Release Key Documents on UFO Office; Cites National Security Risk,' The Black Vault, September 21, 2022; Covert 8)
- 2021, December - A US pilot observes an unusual flashing light that appears to be inconsistent with normal aircraft. 'Unidentified flying object observed from [the] ground with very sporadic flight pattern. Flashing beacon South of [the shore] estimated altitude 3,000-4,000 ft MSL. Object has disappeared twice and reappeared,' the brief

report states. 'Flight pattern [is] too erratic and beyond capabilities of traditional aircraft. Beacon was flashing white. No red/green position lights,' the report concludes. (Micah Hanks, 'Pilot UFO Sightings Collected by NASA Reveal Serious Safety Concerns over Objects in US Airspace,' The Debrief, August 29, 2023)

- 2021, December 3 - 6:00 p.m. A woman driving in downtown Green Bay, Wisconsin, in foggy conditions, spots five lights moving in different directions. The lights merge into one. Three other oval, blue-purple lights also fly through the clouds. She then sees green lights moving very quickly. The sighting lasts 45 minutes and she takes several photos. ('Groups of Multicolored Lights Move Rapidly on Foggy Night,' UFOs Northwest, December 4, 2021)
- 2021, December 9 - The US Defense Department establishes a specialized task force, the Airborne Object Identification and Management Synchronization Group, to investigate and manage UAP sightings and encounters. The group's primary function is to coordinate and synchronize efforts across various military and governmental departments to gather, analyze, and share information regarding UFOs. (John Greenewald, 'Department of Defense Releases AOIMSG-Related Documents,' The Black Vault, May 16, 2023)
- 2021, December 10 - Before 12:00 midnight. Many people in Flawil, St. Gallen, Switzerland, watch a wide bluish light in the sky. Evelyn Zuberbühler-Ehmann snaps a photo that seems to be a reflection, perhaps of a fire below. The light is also seen in Herisau. ( 'Militär, Brand oder UFO: Mysteriöses Licht sorgt weiter für Diskussionen,' Radio FM1Today, St. Gallen, Switzerland, December 14, 2021)
- 2021, December 15 - A new camera system goes into operation at the University of Würzburg, Germany, designed to detect UAP using artificial intelligence. Professor for Space Technology Hakan Kayal has set up SkyCAM-5 on the roof of a university building on the Hubland campus. 'When the camera detects known objects, it recognizes them with a Convolutional Neural Network, classifies them and stores the corresponding video sequences in a database,' Kayal explains. ('UAP: SkyCAM Searches the Sky,' Julius-Maximilians-Universität Würzburg, December 20, 2021)
- 2021, December 27 - President Joe Biden signs into law the National Defense Authorization Act for FY 2022. It includes an amendment, 'Establishment of Office to Address Unidentified Aerial Phenomena' introduced by Sen. Kirsten Gillibrand (D-N.Y.), that requires the Defense Department to coordinate with other federal agencies to collect, analyze, and report on UAP cases, including those with 'adverse physiological effects.' It funds a new office, the Airborne Object Identification and Management Synchronization Group, to replace the UAP Task Force program coordinated by the US Office of Naval Intelligence. The new office will serve as a 'centralized repository' for such information and will coordinate with US allies to 'better assess the nature and extent' of UAP incidents. It requires the office to set up a rapid response team to conduct field investigations and submit an annual report to Congress. ('Sen. Gillibrand Introduces Amendment to Defense Bill Establishing Office to Study UFOs,' WTI-TV, Milwaukee, Wisconsin, November 9, 2021; Kevin D. Randle, 'Coast to Coast AM: Official Study of UFOs and a UFO Picture,' A Different Perspective, November 27, 2021; Douglas Dean Johnson, 'Congress Tells Pentagon and Intelligence Community: UFOs Are Serious Business!' Mirador, December 7, 2021; 'Biden Signs $770 Billion Defense Bill,' New York Times, December 27, 2021; Covert 8-11)

## 2022

- 2022 - Ukrainian ufologists Igor Kalytyuk and Alina Mykolyshyn publish a well-documented, 275-page UFO investigator's field manual in Russian, How to Identify UFOs: How to Investigate UAP , a handbook they have been working on for 9 years. (Igor Kalytyuk and Alina Mykolyshyn, How to Identify UFOs: How to Investigate UAP, 2022)
- 2022-2023 - Three separate instrumented reports of UAP exhibiting unusual 'atmospheric wakes' take place in three US military operations in the Middle East and the Mediterranean Sea. AARO determines that the wake effects are caused by camera or infrared sensor artifacts. (All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office, 'Case: 'Atmospheric Wakes,'' December 2023)
- 2022 - MUFON pays Washington, D.C., lobbying firm A10 Associates $80,000 to lobby Congress on the UAP issue. The firm takes some credit for nudging Congress toward the creation of AARO and public hearings. Ron James, MUFON director of media relations, claims the organization has had at least 100 meetings with legislative staff in which they share data and reports. He admits that MUFON has pushed for AARO to change its historical review of UAP back to 1945 so that the Trinity case could be included. (A10 Associates, 'National Security, UAPs, and the AARO'; Nigel Watson, 'Educating Congress,' Fortean Times, no. 433 (July 2023): 30)

2022, January - Ash Ellis of UFO Identified issues a summary report on 413 UFO sightings made in the UK 2021. The majority originate in England. (Ash Ellis, 'The UK UFO Report 2021,' UFO Identified)

- 2022, January 17 - A military-style drone circles the Forsmark Nuclear Power Plant in Forsmark, Sweden, operating even though there is a high wind. Similar drones with large wings are reported over the Ringhals and Oskarshamn nuclear power plants. The previous week, drones are seen circling the Parliament buildings and the Royal Palace in Stockholm, as well as the Kiruna and Luleå airports. A police helicopter is seen pursuing a drone flying above it at a height of 3,280 feet to the west of Stockholm, but authorities do not manage to down any of these. All the drones seem to be larger and have greater endurance than commercial models. ('Sweden Drones: Sightings Reported over Nuclear Plants and Palace,' BBC News, January 18, 2022; 'Drone Swarms,' Fortean Times, no. 416 (March 2022): 18)
- 2022, January 19 - The CIA announces it has ruled out a sustained global campaign by a hostile power aimed at hundreds of US diplomats and spies as the cause of Havana Syndrome symptoms. In about two dozen cases, the agency cannot rule out foreign involvement, including many of the cases that originated at the US Embassy in Havana, Cuba, beginning in 2016. Another group of cases is considered unresolved. But in hundreds of other cases of possible symptoms, the agency has found plausible alternative explanations. ('CIA Says 'Havana Syndrome' Not Result of Sustained Campaign by Hostile Power,' NBC News, January 19, 2022)
- 2022, January 31 - 11:00 a.m. An unnamed 93-year-old woman is walking along Robert Street in Milford Haven, Wales, when she sees a huge, round, shimmering object dipping in and out of the Milford Haven Waterway. It emits a cloud-like smoke from one side, then from the other side. Then the smoke shoots out of both sides simultaneously, going about 2 feet high. Then the object shoots off into the sky. ('93-year-old among First to Spot Possible UFO in Pembrokeshire,' Pembrokeshire Herald, February 4, 2022)
- 2022, Early February - Night. Isabella Cooper is out for a walk with her father Justin on the Milford Haven side of the Cleddau Estuary. She takes a photo of a blue object several hundred feet in the air above the Valero Pembroke oil refinery. ('Photographer Snaps Possible UFO Sighting in Pembrokeshire Skies,' Pembrokeshire Western Telegraph, February 8, 2022)
- 2022, February 2 - An intelligence panel investigating the cause of Havana Syndrome says that some of the episodes could plausibly have been caused by 'pulsed electromagnetic energy' emitted by an external source, according to a partially declassified intelligence report. A panel of intelligence community experts drew up the report after analyzing over 1,000 documents and interviewing affected individuals. They determine the symptoms associated with the illness to be 'genuine and compelling,' and note that while some cases can be attributed to known psychological or medical factors, others remain unexplained. The authors sought to determine the feasibility of five potential causal mechanisms, including 'acoustic signals, chemical and biological agents, ionizing radiation, natural and environmental factors, and radiofrequency and other electromagnetic energy.' They assessed the potential of each of these mechanisms to account for cases that cannot be easily explained by other means. More specifically, they looked at cases involving a combination of four particularly puzzling 'core characteristics.' These include 'the acute onset of… sound or pressure in only one ear or on one side of the head,' as well as vertigo, 'a strong sense of locality or directionality,' and an absence of any obvious environmental or medical causes for such symptoms. Ruling out the possibility that Havana syndrome could represent an underlying brain disorder, the authors state that 'the combination of the four core characteristics is distinctly unusual and unreported elsewhere in the medical literature, and so far have not been associated with a specific neurological abnormality.' On the other hand, they conclude that 'pulsed electromagnetic energy, particularly in the radiofrequency range, plausibly explains the core characteristics,' although they do concede that such a theory is riddled with 'information gaps.' Addressing the possibility that Havana syndrome could therefore be caused by a nefarious device, they go on to explain that devices do 'exist that could generate the required stimulus, are concealable, and have moderate power requirements' that could produce the observed symptoms. ('Havana Syndrome Could Be Caused by Pulsed Energy Devices, Intelligence Report Says,' IFLScience, February 3, 2022; 'US Intelligence Community Report Says 'Pulsed Electromagnetic Energy' Could Cause Havana Syndrome,' CNN, February 3, 2022; Office of the Director of National Intelligence, [IC Experts Panel on Havana Syndrome executive summary], declassified February 1, 2022; David Hambling, 'Finger on the Pulse?' Fortean Times 426 (Christmas 2022): 14)
- 2022, February 22 - Members of the Pentagon's UAP Task Force, led by USAF Maj. Gen. Daniel L. Simpson, US National Intelligence Manager for Aviation brief 10 Canadian military defense officials, including personnel from the Royal Canadian Air Force and Canadian Forces Intelligence Command. ('Members of Pentagon's UFO Task Force Briefed Canadian Military Officials This Year,' CTV News, November 15, 2022)
- 2022, March 1 - Night. Many witnesses in Ireland contact the media to report a V-shaped object the size of a B-52 moving silently (except for a low buzz or hum) overhead. In Sligo, Leitrim, and Donegal, dozens of people report the object, saying it is low and bright. (Jenny Randles, 'The Return of the Silent Vulcan,' Fortean Times 418 (May 2022): 27)
- 2022, March 2 - National Resources Canada staff appear before a meeting of the House of Commons Standing Committee on National Resources on the Supplementary Estimates. MP Larry Maguire asks a question on incident reporting of drones and UAP, in or near Canadian nuclear facilities. (Keith Basterfield, 'The Canadian Government's Interest in UAP between 2021 and 2024,' Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena-Scientific Research, May 16, 2024)
- 2022, March 3 - 6:45 a.m. A witness in Fox Creek, Alberta, watches an object that seems to have a spiral of light surrounding it. It travels slowly northeast until the spiral vanishes and the light goes out. Possible missile launch. (Chris Rutkowski, 'Some Interesting UFO Sightings Reported in 2022,' Ufology Research, February 6, 2023)
- 2022, March 10 - Evening. Christos Tarsinos and his 15-year-old son watch an object with six white lights move from a nearby hill to the road in front of them, above their car, then back up into the hills. The sighting took place near the Church of St. John on the road to Lygourio, Greece. Tarsinos calls the police, who began watching the lights along with many other villagers and the mayor. ('Mass UFO Sighting in Greece,' Ghost Theory, December 25, 2022)
- 2022, March 14 - National Resources Canada responds to the Standing Committee on Natural Resources's question of March 2. This response is signed off by the Director General, Directorate of Security and Safeguards, who says that the Canadian Nuclear Safety Commission, Canada's independent nuclear regulator, has advised 'there has been no reported drone intrusion or attempted intrusion at Canadian high-security nuclear facilities.' (Keith Basterfield, 'The Canadian Government's Interest in UAP between 2021 and 2024,' Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena-Scientific Research, May 16, 2024)
- 2022, March 25 - The Defense Intelligence Agency releases Defense Intelligence Reference Documents on 37 of the 38 projects that its Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications Program (AAWSAP) has produced as part of its Project Physics under the direction of Hal Puthoff. The topics range from 'Pulsed High-Power Microwave Source Technology' to 'Invisibility Cloaking' and 'Antigravity for Aerospace Applications.' (John Greenewald, 'The Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications Program (AAWSAP) Documentation,' The Black Vault, March 31, 2022)
- 2022, April 12 - 9:30 a.m. NASA Administrator Bill Nelson holds a meeting on UAP in his office, set to last for 90 minutes. The details of the meeting are unknown. (John Greenewald, 'The Black Vault's AAWSAP/AATIP and Post 2017 UAP/UFO Timeline Project,' The Black Vault, January 21, 2023)
- 2022, April 22 - An F-345 pilot sees '8 silver UAS between 16,000 and 20,000 feet' in the vicinity of Glendale, Arizona. (Joseph Trevithick and Tyler Rogoway, 'Pilots Are Seeing Some Very Strange Things in Arizona's Military Training Ranges,' The Drive: The War Zone, August 2, 2023)
- 2022, April 22 - 8:52 p.m. The pilot of an Embraer Phenom business jet flying at 45,000 feet above Kessel, West Virginia, notices a light 10,000 feet above him. It changes to a string of lights. As he passes underneath, he notices the object's lights go out and the aircraft's avionics system fails. Other pilots can see the light as well. Probable Starlink satellite deployment.  (John Greenewald, 'FAA Confirmed UFO Sighting April 22, 2022, by LXJ359 over Kessel, West Virginia,' The Black Vault, May 19, 2022; Groupe d'Études d'Information sur les Phénomènes Aérospatiaux Non-identifiés, 'Note d'enquête: Observations liées a Falcon 9,' July 4, 2022)
- 2022, May - During a routine training flight, a US pilot instructor is forced to take evasive action to avoid a midair collision with an unknown object. 'While we were clearing the area for a maneuver I spotted the unidentified object as we were quickly approaching it,' the pilot's report states. The object reportedly appears very suddenly and appears to be hovering without showing any signs of movement. 'I immediately took the controls and took evasive action in order to avoid a midair collision,' the pilot states in the report. 'Initially, I mistook the object for something of the light weight [sic] category or a drone, since it did not resemble an airplane or a helicopter.' 'As we passed the object I was able to get a [decent] look at the object and could not identify what [it] could possibly be. I then resumed the training flight in a safe direction away from this encounter.' (Micah Hanks, 'Pilot UFO Sightings Collected by NASA Reveal Serious Safety Concerns over Objects in US Airspace,' The Debrief, August 29, 2023)
- 2022, May 12 - Department of Defense officials choose Deputy Director of Intelligence and physicist Sean Kirkpatrick to head the Airborne Object Identification and Management Synchronization Group (AOIMSG). (Douglas Dean Johnson, 'Scientist and Intelligence Officer Sean Kirkpatrick Picked to Head the New Pentagon-IC Office

Empowered by Congress to Study Unidentified Aerial Phenomena,' Mirador, May 12, 2022; Jane Edwards, 'Sean Kirkpatrick Named Director of DOD All-Domain Anomaly Resolution Office,' Executive.Gov, July 21, 2022)

- 2022, May 12 - Larry Maguire, Member of Parliament for Brandon-Souris, Manitoba, calls for the Canadian Parliament to engage on the topic of UFOs in a 'serious and thoughtful manner' and make information available openly and transparently. (Larry Maguire, 'UAPs Are Real, and Canada Should Take Them Seriously,' The Line, May 12, 2022)
- 2022, May 14 - A US military Reaper drone takes a 7-second video and six images of a cigar-shaped object north of Baghdad, Iraq, in an active combat zone. (John Greenewald, 'The 'Baghdad Phantom UAP' Imagery Published by Jeremy Corbell and George Knapp,' The Black Vault, March 7, 2023)
- 2022, May 17 - 9:00 a.m. The House Intelligence Committee's Counterterrorism, Counterintelligence, and Counterproliferation Subcommittee holds hearings on Unidentified Aerial Phenomena, as directed by the National Defense Authorization Act, which calls on the military to provide an annual report and semiannual briefings on the topic to Congress. The hearing features Ronald Moultrie, under secretary of defense for intelligence and security, who is involved with the newly created Airborne Object Identification and Management Synchronization Group (AOIMSG), and Scott W. Bray, deputy director of naval intelligence. Bray testifies that their database of reports of UFOs now includes about 400 incidents, up from 143 assessed in a report released in 2021. He cites improved sensors, an increase in drones and other non-military unmanned aerial systems, and aerial clutter such as Mylar balloons as causes for the uptick. Incidents in the 2021 report date as far back as 2004 and are based on both sensor data and observations by military aviators. Bray says that 'Navy and air force crews now have stepby-step procedures for reporting UAPs on their kneeboard, in the cockpit.' In a back-and-forth with Rep. Brad Wenstrup (R-Ohio), Bray agrees that standardizing the civilian reporting process will be useful. While the military database does include some civilian reports, the vast majority have come from within. Rep. Adam Schiff (D-Calif.), the chairman of the House Intelligence Committee, pushes for the Pentagon and the public to understand that UAPs are becoming a national security concern. At the hearing, officials play a declassified video clip showing a mysterious UAP that zipped by a pilot's aircraft in a US Navy training yard. It appears to be a spherical object traveling at extremely high speeds. Bray says that he does not have an explanation for what this specific object is. Bray and Moultrie both say they will commit to declassifying more information when possible and when it does not pose a national security risk, adding the task force will operate with more transparency than past Pentagon programs. ('Unidentified Aerial Phenomena,' transcript of a hearing by the House Subcommittee on Counterterrorism, Counterintelligence, and Counterproliferation, May 17, 2022; 'Watch: Congressional Hearing on UFOs!: Live,' CNET Highlights YouTube channel, May 17, 2022; Christopher Dean Hopkins, 'The Military's UFO Database Now Has Info from About 400 Reported Incidents,' National Public Radio, May 17, 2022; Brad Dress, 'UFOs Pose 'Potential National Security Threat,' Lawmakers Warn,' The Hill, May 17, 2022; Luis Martinez, 'Pentagon Now Reports about 400 UFO Encounters,' ABC News, May 17, 2022)
- 2022, May 25 - National Geospacial-Intelligence Agency David Grusch submits a complaint of reprisal to the Department of Defense Inspector General about adverse security clearance actions and other reprisals related to his confidential briefing of UAP-related classified information to the IG. He requests permission to communicate classified specifics of his previous UAP information to the Senate and House Select Committees on Intelligence. (Irvin Charles McCullough III and Andrew P. Bakaj to the Intelligence Community Inspector General, 'Disclosure of Urgent Concern(s): Complaint of Reprisal,' May 25, 2022)
- 2022, May 27 - The Center for UFO Studies launches its first Facebook page. (CUFOS, Facebook page)
- 2022, June 6 - John Hannaford, Deputy Minister of Natural Resources Canada, and Kathleen Heppell-Masys, Director General for the Directorate of Security and Safeguards of the Canada Nuclear Safety Commission, write to MP Larry Maguire in two separate letters as a follow-up to his question in Parliament about UAP sightings near Canadian nuclear facilities. Both deny any such incidents. ((Keith Basterfield, 'The Canadian Government's Interest in UAP between 2021 and 2024,' Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena-Scientific Research, May 16, 2024)
- 2022, June 9 - NASA announces that it is commissioning a study team to examine UAP reports from a scientific perspective for nine months. The study will focus on identifying available data, how best to collect future data, and how NASA can use that data to move the scientific understanding of UAPs forward. It will be led by astrophysicist and cosmologist David Spergel, president of the Simons Foundation in New York City and previously the chair of the astrophysics department at Princeton University in Princeton, New Jersey. Daniel Evans, the assistant deputy associate administrator for research at NASA's Science Mission Directorate, will serve as the NASA official responsible for orchestrating the study. The agency hopes to gather data on 'events in

the sky that cannot be identified as aircraft or known natural phenomena-from a scientific perspective.' A first step for the team will be to attempt to establish which UAPs are natural phenomena. Swiss-American astrophysicist Thomas Zurbuchen gives an 'Update and Discussion with NASA Science Mission Directorate' presentation at the joint Aeronautics and Space Engineering Board and Space Studies Board meeting at the National Academy of Sciences. Information about UAP is included, particularly about NASA commissioning an independent study on UAP. (Katie Hunt and Ashley Strickland, 'NASA Is Assembling a Team to Gather Data on Unidentifiable Events in the Sky,' CNN, June 9, 2022; 'UAP: NASA Unidentified Aerial Phenomena Independent Study,' NASA Science; John Greenewald, 'Dr. Tomas Zurbuchen/NASA Slides Relating to UAP/UFO Program, June 2022,' The Black Vault, June 11, 2022)

- 2022, June 21 - George Knapp with KLAS-TV in Las Vegas publishes the identity of one of the scientists working with the UAP Task Force. Now a disbanded effort to pave the way for the Airborne Object Identification and Management Synchronization Group (AOIMSG), Travis Taylor is said to have been the 'chief scientist.' Taylor played a role in 'analyzing the 144 incidents, including dozens of bizarre objects that buzzed multiple Navy ships in 2019.' (John Greenewald, 'Pentagon Releases Details about Dr. Travis Taylor's UAP Task Force Involvement,' The Black Vault, June 29, 2022)
- 2022, June 22 - 4:00 a.m. Philosophy instructor Carlos Paz Morales is driving north from Cafayate to Salta, Argentina, when a light comes flooding in through the rear window of his truck. He pulls onto the shoulder of ther oad near a rock formation known as El Sapo. He gets out and sees a large, white object in the hills not far away. It is cigarshaped and moves from left to right in a zig-zag pattern. For the next 5 minutes he watches the object and takes some photos with his cellphone, then it vanishes in an instant. ('Argentina: Teacher from Salta Claims Seeing UFO on the Way to Cafayate,' Inexplicata: The Journal of Hispanic Ufology, June 24, 2022)
- 2022, June 23 -The US Senate Select Committee on Intelligence approves the Fiscal Year 2023 Intelligence Authorization Act (S. 4503), which contains 31 pages of revamped and expanded UAP requirements, including provisions crafted  to elicit fuller cooperation by the Director of National Intelligence and all intelligence agencies, and creation of a 'secure system' to encourage current or past government and contractor employees with UFO data to channel that information to the central UAP office, and ultimately to Congress, without incurring legal jeopardy or risking job reprisals. The bill would also order both a government-wide search for UAP-related Non-Disclosure Agreements (NDAs), and a comprehensive study of intelligence agency UFO involvement going back to 1947, the latter to be conducted by the Government Accountability Office. ('Sec. 1683. Establishment of Aerospace-Undersea Phenomena Joint Program Office,' S. 4503, July 12, 2022, pp. 87117; Douglas Dean Johnson, 'Congressional Intelligence and Armed Services Committees Advance Measures to Probe for Possible Hidden UFO Data,' Mirador, July 14, 2022)
- 2022, July 6 - 5:06 p.m. A witness in Spruce Grove, Alberta, is followed by a black, triangular object that buzzes him then flies away. Later, pieces of what appears to be molten metal are found on the ground where he had been standing. (Chris Rutkowski, 'Some Interesting UFO Sightings Reported in 2022,' Ufology Research, February 6, 2023)
- 2022, July 7 - John and Gerald Tedesco, of Harvard's Galileo Project, begin a year-long instrumented field study at Robert Moses State Park on Long Island, New York, to observe and record light phenomena of an unknown nature. The team deploys a 3cm X-band radar operating in two scan geometries, as well as multispectral electrooptical devices. On numerous occasions they observe a light phenomenon that 'most often presented itself as a spheroid under a luminous state, while under decreased luminosity, it seemed to display a polyhedral appearance morphologically. Fewer sightings appeared as ovaloid and cylindroid.' The lights appear consistently from July 7. 2022, through March 30, 2023, and display fluctuating states of albedo, glinting, rotation, variations in spectral range, and 'change in state from luminous to illuminous.' At times it demonstrates swarm-like behavior that cannot be verified by Automatic Dependent Surveillance-Broadcast data. (John Joseph Tedesco and Gerald Thomas Tedesco, 'Eye on the Sky: A UAP Research and Field Study off New York's Long Island Coast,' Open Journal of Applied Sciences 14, no. 8 (August 2024): 2267-2295; Baptiste Friscourt, 'Engineers Claim to Have Recorded UAP in New Paper,' Sentinel News Substack, August 28. 2024)
- 2022, July 12 - A video taken by an MQ-9 Reaper drone above Deir ez-Zor, Syria, shows a silver ball crossing the sensor's field of view. AARO's Sean Kirkpatrick gives this as an example of a sighting with limited data (only one source of instrumentation) that AARO is still assessing. He adds that it does not exhibit any anomalous behavior. An independent analysis of the video by Nathan Patin and Logan Williams confirms that the object is most likely a balloon. Relying on structures that appeared in the video, the pair were able to use Google Earth measurements to produce an estimate for the size of the small, metallic sphere as approximately 1.5 feet in diameter or smaller. Patin and Williams propose that while the object appears to be moving in the video, it is

possible that it is actually remaining stationary and that the apparent motion is the result of parallax. (John Greenewald, 'DoD Releases New UFO/UAP Video from an Undisclosed Location-July 12, 2022,' The Black Vault, April 19, 2023; Nathan Patin and Logan Williams, 'Isn't That a Balloon? Deflating a DoD UFO Video,' Bellingcat, October 24, 2023; Micah Hanks, 'Has the Mystery behind One of the U.S. Military's UAP Videos Been Solved?' The Debrief, October 26, 2023)

- 2022, July 13 - The US House of Representatives votes on its own version of the 2023 Intelligence Authorization Act, which includes a provision to create a secure government system for reporting UFOs and to compel current and former officials to reveal what they might know about the mysterious phenomena by promising to protect them from reprisal. (Bryan Bender and Lawrence Ukenye, 'House Votes to Make It Easier to Report UFOs,' Politico, July 13, 2022)
- 2022, July 15 - Under Secretary of Defense Ronald Moultrie informs the Department of Defense of the renaming and expansion of the scope of the Airborne Object Identification and Management Group (AOIMSG) to the Alldomain Anomaly Resolution Office (AARO), due to the enactment of the National Defense Authorization Act for fiscal 2022 and appoints Sean M. Kirkpatrick, most recently the chief scientist at the Defense Intelligence Agency's Missile and Space Intelligence Center, as the director of AARO. (John Greenewald, 'DoD Announces the Establishment of the All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office,' The Black Vault, July 20, 2022; Ronald S. Moultrie, 'Establishment, Resourcing, and Leadership of the All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office,' memorandum to senior Pentagon leadership, July 20, 2022; Keith Basterfield, 'Establishment of the US Department of Defence All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office,' Unidentified Anomalous PhenomenaScientific Research, July 27, 2022; Covert, xxx)
- 2022, July 20 - The Senate Select Committee on Intelligence approves its explanatory Committee Report on S 4503. The report contains a short section in which the committee rebukes the Department of Defense for the pace at which the Department has implemented the requirements of the December 2021 Gillibrand-Rubio-Gallego UAP law. The House Intelligence Committee approves the FY 2023 Intelligence Authorization Act, which includes language about unidentified aerospace-undersea phenomena. (US Senate Select Committee on Intelligence, 'Intelligence Authorization Act for Fiscal Year 2023: Report Together with Additional Views to Accompany S. 4503,' July 20, 2022; Douglas Dean Johnson, 'Congressional Intelligence and Armed Services Committees Advance Measures to Probe for Possible Hidden UFO Data,' Mirador, July 14, 2022; John Greenewald, 'Unidentified Aerospace-Undersea Phenomena Section in Approved Fiscal Year 2023 Intelligence Authorization Act,' The Black Vault, July 20, 2022)
- 2022, July 22 - 10:25 p.m. A married couple resting in their outdoor garden in Calangianus, Sardinia, notice a silent, illuminated object moving south to north above their house. It is oblong with sharp edges. The interior seems to have a sparkling light like a disco strobe. They see two luminous points on it that at first seem to flicker, but when they look at it through binoculars, the lights seem to be rotating. The object's speed is constant and similar to that of an airliner. A photograph accompanies the report, but there is no mention of one being taken. (Stefano Innocenti, 'Ancora Ufo della Sardegna: Interessante Avvistamento nella Provincia di Sassari,' Il Faro (Fiumicino, Italy), July 30, 2022)
- 2022, July 31 - Just before 12:00 midnight. A bizarre metal orb emblazoned with a secret code and perhaps containing 'valuable information inside' falls from the sky onto a tree in Veracruz, Mexico, creating a firestorm of intrigue on social media and in local media. Meteorologist Isidro Cano Luna calls on the Mexican Navy to study the object. (Jason Koebler, 'Mysterious Metallic Orb Falls on Mexico, May Contain 'Valuable Information,' Meteorologist Says,' Vice, August 1, 2022)
- 2022, August - Researchers B. E. Zhilyaev, V. N. Petukhov, and V. M. Reshetnyk of the Main Astronomical Observatory of NAS of Ukraine announce they have undertaken a study of UAPs reported in the country. The team says it employs a pair of meteor stations located in Kyiv and in Vinarivka village, each fitted with video cameras observing the daytime sky. The researchers say they have created 'a special observation technique' they use 'for detecting and evaluating UAP characteristics.' Based on the events they have observed, they break down their observations of UAP into two primary categories, which they call 'Cosmics' (nocturnal lights) and 'Phantoms' (dark objects). (Micah Hanks, 'Cosmics and Phantoms: Ukrainian Independent Study Reveals Observations of Unidentified Aerial Phenomena,' The DeBrief, August 26, 2022)
- 2022, August 3 - 10:45 a.m. A witness is driving down his driveway in a rural area of Oxford, Connecticut, when he sees a 'bipedal creature about 4 feet tall' run out of the woods to his left, move across the driveway about 30 feet in  front of him, and take off into the woods. The creature is brown, skinny, with no fur or hair, and looks like it is not even touching the ground. (Rich Kirby, 'Why Has There Been an Increase in UFO Sightings in CT?' Patch Media, Across Connecticut, January 2, 2023)
- 2022, August 6-September 23 - Pilots of commercial airliners report sightings of multiple UFOs moving in a circular path like a 'racetrack.' Ben Hansen collects raw footage and air traffic control recordings documenting several of these events. (''Racetrack UAPs' Reported by Dozens of Pilots and Commercial Flights,' Hypocenter 101 YouTube channel, October 6, 2022; Patrick Reilly, 'Several Recent UFO Sightings Reported by Pilots over the Pacific Ocean,' New York Post, October 20, 2022)
- 2022, August 10 - 4:30 a.m. A witness in Vicksburg, Michigan, takes several photos of a cylindrical light in the sky. Others also report the object. (Chelsea Rose, 'Unidentified Flying Object Seen in Vicksburg: Can You Explain It?' WITL-FM, Lansing, Michigan, August 11, 2022)
- 2022, August 12 - 12:55 a.m. The pilot of a commercial airliner flying north near Wilmington, North Carolina, observes 2 or 3 objects making regular pulses of light for 8-10 seconds each. He watches them for 35 minutes, during which time he manages to take several videos of them with his cellphone. As a former military pilot, he states that the objects are flying too high for most commercial aircraft and they do not appear to be military. Skeptic Mick West suggests that this may be a Starlink satellite deployment. (David Hookstead, 'Pilot Films Alleged UFO Encounter Lasting More Than 30 Minutes,' OutKick, August 22, 2022; Mick West, 'Why 'Racetrack' UFOs Are Mostly Starlink Flares,' Mick West YouTube channel, August 19, 2022; Mick West, 'MUFON Report 124374,' Metabunk, August 19, 2022)
- 2022, August 14 - 8:00 p.m. Martha Santana takes some cellphone footage of a strange glowing object, sometimes round, other times conical, that maneuvers for several minutes in Mañongo, Venezuela. It moves up and down, then hovers, increasing in brightness at each end. (Jackson Camargo, 'OVNI brilhante é filmado na Venezuela,' Portal Fenomenum, September 5, 2022)
- 2022, August 23 - 9:00-11:00 p.m. A man driving on US Highway 23 north of Circleville, Ohio, observes a number of strange lights that appear to descend, hut the ground, and rise back up. At least six people report the lights over a two-hour period. ('UFOs Spotted in Circleville,' Circleville (Ohio) Herald, August 26, 2022)
- 2022, August 28 - A witness in Louisville, Kentucky, notices a hexagon-shaped object hovering some 300 feet above his house. He is nearly directly underneath it as it slowly drifts off to the northeast. It has a round bottom and yellowish, reddish, and orange lights, as well as four dim white lights, all steady, in a hexagon shape on its underside. He can only hear a slight humming sound. It is about 60-80 feet in diameter. (Kevin D. Randle, 'Coast-to-Coast AM: The Lubbock Lights,' A Different Perspective, February 23, 2023)
- 2022, September 1 - NASA astronomers report that the James Webb Space Telescope has taken the first photograph of an exoplanet, one that is circling the small star HIP 65426. (Alfredo Carpineti, 'JWST Snaps Its First Image of an Exoplanet-And It's a Very Weird World,' IFLScience, September 1, 2022)
- 2022, September 1 - Night. Gus McGiven and Kyle Gomez are walking in the Cat Hollow Park area of Brushy Creek, Texas, when they spot a circular formation of five white lights. Emily White also records a video clip of the silent formation in the same area. ('Witnesses, Drone Expert React to Mysterious Lights Captured in Texas Sky,' KTBC-TV, Austin, Texas, September 2, 2022)
- 2022, September 7 - The UAP Task Force responds to a request by the Black Vault for information on all UFO videos in the possession of the US Navy, claiming that 'the requested videos contain sensitive information pertaining to Unidentified Aerial Phenomena (UAP) and are classified and are exempt from disclosure in their entirety.' (John Greenewald, 'U.S. Navy Says All UAP/UFO Videos Are Classified and Exempt from Release,' The Black Vault, September 8, 2022)
- 2022, September 10 - 8:00 p.m. Witnesses sailing on the Fraser River near Vancouver, British Columbia, see a large, disc-shaped object, with a mirror-like finish on its underside, hovering above their sailboat. (Chris Rutkowski, 'Some Interesting UFO Sightings Reported in 2022,' Ufology Research, February 6, 2023)
- 2022, September 24 - 7:00 p.m. Witnesses driving at Sainte-Martine, Quebec, watch a group of bright lights hovering above trees along the horizon. ('UFO Ste-Martine,' OVNI-Expert; Chris Rutkowski, 'Some Interesting UFO Sightings Reported in 2022,' Ufology Research, February 6, 2023)
- 2022, September 27 - 5:25 a.m. Multiple flashing dim lights are reported near Liberal, Kansas, by a Gulfstream IV twinjet pilot and other aircraft for 35 minutes. The original pilot describes them 'on the ground, seemingly everywhere.' However, it appears the pilot is at about 35,000 feet, and at one point seems to say that the lights have followed him and pace the aircraft for at least 50 miles. (John Greenewald, 'FAA Confirms 'UAP' Seen by Numerous Pilots over Kansas, September 27, 2022,' The Black Vault, November 25, 2022)
- 2022, October 7 - 10:30 p.m. An aircraft reports a UFO 5.7 miles south of La Crosse (Wis.) Regional Airport flying at 1,500 feet. The airport's air traffic control also sees the object, which has alternating green, red, and blue lights and appears to 'look like a jellyfish.' It is traveling westbound and shows up as a strong radar target. (John

Greenewald, 'FAA Confirms 'Jellyfish'-Like 'Large Unknown Object' over Minneapolis, Minnesota-October 7, 2022,' The Black Vault, December 20, 2022)

- 2022, October 9 - 6:00 p.m. A MADAR surveillance node in Newington, Connecticut, alerts its manager, who goes outside for a look. At first the only thing that he sees is a few airplanes flying by, but as he looks through his binoculars at one of the planes, he spots a small, round, black object descending from the clouds above the airplane and moving back up. It repeats this maneuver twice. (Rich Kirby, 'Why Has There Been an Increase in UFO Sightings in CT?' Patch Media, Across Connecticut, January 2, 2023)
- 2022, October 13 - A doorbell camera in Raytown, Missouri, captures a slowly moving image of an apparently structured object that sometimes seems translucent and at other times reflects light (possibly an effect of rotation). (''Mind-Blowing' UFO Footage Captured by Doorbell Camera,' WBZ-AM, Boston, Massachusetts, October 18, 2022)
- 2022, October 19 - The American Institute of Aeronautics and Astronautics establishes three committees to study the technology of UAPs, to study how UAP incursions affect pilot and passenger safety, and to coordinate with government agencies and international researchers also focused on the topic. (''New Territory': America's Top Aerospace Sleuths Join UFO Hunt,' Politico, October 19, 2022)
- 2022, October 21 - NASA selects 16 individuals to participate in its independent study team on UAP, led by David Spergel. Among them are oceanographer Paula Bontempi, journalist Nadia Drake, astrobiologist David Grinspoon, former astronaut Scott Kelly, and astronomer Matt Mountain. (John Greenewald, 'NASA Announces Unidentified Aerial Phenomena Study Team Members,' The Black Vault, October 21, 2022)
- 2022, October 24 - NASA launches an international study team that will establish the groundwork for a scientific study of UAP reports over the next 9 months. The team will identify how data gathered by civilian government entities, commercial data, and data from other sources can potentially be analyzed to shed light on UAPs. It will then recommend a roadmap for potential data analysis by the agency. The study will focus solely on unclassified data and release a full report on its findings in mid-2023. The NASA official responsible for orchestrating the study is Daniel Evans, assistant deputy associate administrator for research at NASA's Science Mission Directorate. The team itself is chaired by David Spergel, president of the Simons Foundation. (John Greenewald, 'NASA Announces Unidentified Aerial Phenomena Team Members,' The Black Vault, October 21, 2022)
- 2022, November 9 - UAPx, a private group that specializes in investigating UAP and 'providing direct access to the general public of reviewed and analyzed data' presents a private briefing on UFOs to the House Armed Services Committee's Intelligence and Special Operations Subcommittee. The group was co-founded by Kevin Day, a retired US Navy Senior Chief Petty Officer who was on board the USS Princeton during the November 2004 'tictac' encounter. Although Day has since moved on to the other endeavors, the group continues to be led by its other co-founder and president, Gary Voorhis. Some of the remaining team members of UAPx include Kevin Knuth, a former NASA research scientist and physicist who is currently an associate professor in the Department of Physics at the University at Albany (SUNY); Matthew Szydagis, a physicist and associate professor at the University at Albany Department of Physics pursuing experimental particle astrophysics; and Cecilia Levy, an assistant professor in the Department of Physics at the University of Albany, who like the rest of UAPx, believes UAP should not be a taboo topic, but rather, receive serious scientific investigation. (John Greenewald, 'Exclusive: UAPx Offers Sneak Peek at Their UAP Briefing Given to Congressional Committee,' The Black

Vault, November 11, 2022)

- 2022, November 10 - 8:48 p.m. The pilot of a Piper PA-28 Cherokee aircraft reports an object with a bright rotating white light about the size of a Cessna C172 Skyhawk moving swiftly at 4,000 feet about 11.5 miles southwest of La Grange, Texas. He estimates the object to be 1-2 miles away to the southwest. Possible fireball meteor. (John Greenewald, ''Very Bright…Rotating White Light' UFO over Texas Described by Pilot in FAA ATC Recordings,' The Black Vault, January 3, 2023)
- 2022, November 11 - 12:42 p.m. The pilot of a commercial airliner flying over Saint-Hyacinthe, Quebec, spots a drone flying uncomfortably close to his aircraft. The flight is at 9,000 feet and the crew watches as the drone flies within 200-300 feet off the left wingtip. (Chris Rutkowski, 'A Curious Collection of Canadian CADORS Cases,' Ufology Research, December 1, 2022)
- 2022, November 12 - 3:21 a.m. Pilots of a Boeing 777 cargo plane flying over Kenabeek, Ontario, see lights flying eastward at the same speed as their aircraft. (Chris Rutkowski, 'A Curious Collection of Canadian CADORS Cases,' Ufology Research, December 1, 2022)
- 2022, November 13 - 3:21 a.m. An Aerologic Boeing 777 cargo plane flying from Chicago to Frankfurt, Germany, reports seeing lights that are moving eastward over Kenabeek, Ontario, at the same speed as the aircraft.

(CADORS Report 2022O2794; Chris Rutkowski, 'A Curious Collection of Canadian CADORS Cases,' Ufology Research, December 1, 2022)

- 2022, November 14 - 6:49 p.m. An Italian Air Force soldier and her daughter are just outside the Castel di Leva area in Rome, Italy, when they notice two stars clearly visible near the planet Jupiter. They are stationary. She takes two photos with her cellphone, and when she looks up again the lights have faded. She takes more photos, including Jupiter as a point of reference. After a few seconds, the lights have disappeared. After returning home, he enhances the photos on her computer and finds that the two lights appear to be on a single, black, conical or discshaped object. The next day, she returns to the site and, using triangulation incorporating the focal length of the cellphone, she estimates that the object was 750 feet high, 65 feet in diameter, and less than a quarter-mile away. The Centro Italiano Studi Ufologici studies the photos and suggests that the object was actually two balloons with LED lights inside. (Stefano Innocenti, 'Due luci vicino give a Roma nel 2022: Per ora sono due Ufo,' Il Faro (Fiumicino, Italy), April 2, 2024)
- 2022, November 18 - David Marler establishes the National UFO Historical Record Center in Rio Rancho, New Mexico, 'dedicated to the preservation and centralization of UFO/UAP information in the United States.' Its mission is to collect, preserve, and provide historical UFO materials to the general public and interested parties. Marler is the executive director, along with a team of UFO historians and archivists that includes Jan Aldrich, Rod Dyke, Barry Greenwood, Mark Rodeghier, and Rob Swiatek. Marler's home already houses one of the largest private collections of UFO records in the country. Since the early 1990s, he has acquired thousands of civilian documents, audio recordings, videotapes of reported UFO sightings, and hundreds of declassified government documents. NUFOHRC currently houses the archives of the Center for UFO Studies, the National Investigations Committee on Aerial Phenomena, and numerous cases and documents from the collections of more than 70 other individuals in the US and overseas. The group is working to acquire a building that can hold a library, workspace, and a public museum. (Micah Hanks, 'Nation's Largest Center for Historical Records on UFOs to Be Established in New Mexico,' The Debrief, November 18, 2022; 'National UFO Record Archive Planned for NM,' KOB-TV, Albuquerque, New Mexico, November 26, 2022; ; Ryan Boetel, 'Rio Rancho Man Spent Decades Building Collection of UFO Items, But He's Not Trying to Make Anyone Believe,' Albuquerque (N.Mex.) Journal, December 4, 2022)
- 2022, December 9 - 9:40 p.m. A witness in Pottstown, Pennsylvania, sees a silent, triangle-shaped object with three dim orange lights moving slowly near the Limerick Generating Station on the Schuylkill River. The object heads south, then turns west at the plant. ('Witness Report Claims UFO near Pennsylvania Nuclear Power Plant,' NorthCentralPA.com, January 27, 2023)
- 2022, December 13 - The pilot of an F-35 out of Luke AFB flying in the eastern end of a section of restricted airspace above the Barry M. Goldwater Air Force Range in southwest Arizona observes four small objects slowly moving eastbound at 20,000 feet. The pilot takes evasive action. About one hour later, a second F-35 pilot has two separate encounters, the first with a single object and the second with eight small objects, above the same space. (Joseph Trevithick and Tyler Rogoway, 'Pilots Are Seeing Some Very Strange Things in Arizona's Military Training Ranges,' The Drive: The War Zone, August 2, 2023)
- 2022, December 14 - The pilot of an F-35 on a training sortie in the southeastern portion of the Barry M. Goldwater Air Force Range in southwest Arizona sees a 'small black, and metallic object' after detecting it on radar. (Joseph Trevithick and Tyler Rogoway, 'Pilots Are Seeing Some Very Strange Things in Arizona's Military Training Ranges,' The Drive: The War Zone, August 2, 2023)
- 2022, December 15 - The pilots of two F-35s detect a UFO, possibly through radar alone, while flying at 16,000 feet through the Gladden and Sells Military Operations Areas, respectively, in Arizona. (Joseph Trevithick and Tyler Rogoway, 'Pilots Are Seeing Some Very Strange Things in Arizona's Military Training Ranges,' The Drive: The War Zone, August 2, 2023)
- 2022, December 16 - Under Secretary of Defense Ronald Moultrie and AARO director Sean M. Kirkpatrick conduct a press briefing on the Pentagon's UAP study (an unadvertised, invitation-only, one-hour event), revealing that the office has received hundreds of new reports that do not necessarily represent an increase since May 2022. AARO has 'not seen anything that would lead us to believe that any of the objects that we have seen are of alien origin,' Moultrie states. 'If we find something like that, we will look at it and analyze it and take the appropriate actions.' openly refers to the range of phenomena being studied by the All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office for the first time publicly as 'unidentified anomalous phenomena,' rather than unidentified aerial phenomena as has been seen prevalently in recent years. Moultrie also states that AARO presently has not come across any credible data indicating transmedium capabilities displayed by UAP. He also says that many of these cases are not considered dangerous and may end up being 'things like balloons and things like UAVs that are operated

for purposes other than surveillance or intelligence collection.' (Transcript, 'USD(I&S) Ronald Moultrie and Dr. Sean Kirkpatrick Media Roundtable on the All-domain Anomaly Research Office,' US Department of Defense, December 16, 2022; Howard Altman, 'Pentagon's New UFO Office: No Aliens But Many Threat Concerns,' The Drive: War Zone, December 16, 2022; Oren Lieberman, 'Pentagon Offers Few Answers in UFO Investigation But Has Received Several Hundred More Reports,' CNN, December 16, 2022; John Greenewald, ''Media Roundtable' Transcript about the Pentagon's UFO Office, AARO-December 16, 2022,' The Black Vault, December 17, 2022)

- 2022, December 23 - President Joe Biden signs a defense appropriations bill that includes funding for the All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office (extending its authority to examine cases back to 1945) and protection for whistleblowers reporting UAP information. It also calls for any non-disclosure agreements related to UAP in past or existing DOD, DHS, and other programs to be shared with AARO leadership. (Micah Hanks, '$858 Billion Defense Bill Signed into Law by Biden Includes 'Unprecedented' Legislation on 'Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena,' The Debrief, December 24, 2022)

## 2023

- 2023, January 5 - The pilot of a 56th Fighter Wing F-35 conducting a training sortie in the Gladden Military Operations Area in Arizona makes radar contact with a UFO at 18,700 feet. (Joseph Trevithick and Tyler Rogoway, 'Pilots Are Seeing Some Very Strange Things in Arizona's Military Training Ranges,' The Drive: The War Zone, August 2, 2023)
- 2023, January 12 - The Office of the Director of National Intelligence releases its second UAP report to the general public. It was originally due on October 31, 2022, but has been delayed. Among the most notable takeaways in the report, its authors tell us that 'In addition to the 144 UAP reports covered during the 17 years of UAP reporting included in the Office of the Director of National Intelligence (ODNI) preliminary assessment, there have been 247 new reports and another 119 that were either since discovered or reported after the preliminary assessment's time period. This totals 510 UAP reports as of 30 August 2022. Additional information is provided in the classified version of this report,' the authors state.' 510 total UAP reports, which includes 26 characterized as Unmanned Aircraft System (UAS) or UAS-like entities, 163 characterized as balloon or balloon-like entities, and 6 attributed to clutter.' That leaves a grand total of 171 reports that remain 'uncharacterized and unattributed,' and which 'appear to have demonstrated unusual flight characteristics or performance capabilities and require further analysis.' Page 6 of the unclassified report features a short section addressing flight safety and health issues associated with UAP; although there have been no confirmed health issues, the report hints there may have been suspected issues.  (John Greenewald, 'U.S. Government Releases 2nd UAP/UFO Report to the Public,' The Black Vault, January 12, 2023; Micah Hanks, 'The 2022 Annual Report on UAP: Four Significant Takeaways You Probably Missed,' The DeBrief, January 19, 2023)
- 2023, January 15 - Footage that shows an object with an apparent atmospheric wake or cavitation trailing it is taken on infrared film by an MQ-9 Reaper drone in an undisclosed location in South Asia. AARO's Sean M. Kirkpatrick says that the object at first appeared anomalous, but after further study, AARO identifies it as a commercial aircraft and the cavitation is a sensor artifact resulting from video compression. (John Greenewald, 'DoD Releases New UFO/UAP Videos They Believe They Identified-South Asia-01/15/2023,' The Black Vault, April 19, 2023)
- 2023, January 19 - An F-16 Viper fighter jet flying in the Barry M. Goldwater Air Force Range in Arizona collides in mid-air with an 'orange-white UAS.' The supposed drone strikes the fighter's canopy. (Joseph Trevithick and Tyler Rogoway, 'Pilots Are Seeing Some Very Strange Things in Arizona's Military Training Ranges,' The Drive: The War Zone, August 2, 2023)
- 2023, January 20 - An F-35 flying out of Luke AFB is conducting a training sortie in restricted airspace above the Barry M. Goldwater Air Force Range in Arizona. It has three encounters with small unknown objects. The first is detected at 20,000 feet in the northeastern section of the Sells Military Operations Area traveling eastbound at 100 knots. The second is detected at 33,000 feet traveling westbound at 0.75 Mach. The third is detected at 26,000 feet, traveling eastbound at approximately 100 knots. (Joseph Trevithick and Tyler Rogoway, 'Pilots Are Seeing Some Very Strange Things in Arizona's Military Training Ranges,' The Drive: The War Zone, August 2, 2023)
- 2023, January 26 - A US Air Force pilot on a sortie out of Eglin AFB, southwest of Valparaiso, Florida, gains a radar lock on four objects in a diamond formation over the Gulf of Mexico. Upon approach, the pilot sees the lead object, which is roughly pear-shaped and the size of an Apollo spacecraft, and obtains a 'screen capture' of it. Flying at 16,000 feet, it seems to be composed of gunmetal-gray segmented panels with an orange-reddish

illuminated section in the lower center. The other objects, detected only on radar, are at altitudes of 17,00018,000 feet. Upon approaching within 4,000 feet of the first object, the pilot's radar, video, infrared, and other aircraft security features malfunction; a declassified report indicates that a circuit breaker was triggered. An unreleased video apparently exists because a still photo showing the object is apparently a still frame from a video, and a drawing of the object is released in March 2024. The pilot contacts Rep. Matt Gaetz (R-Fla.) about the incident. Gaetz is later briefed on the incident at Eglin. A report on the incident is eventually sent on to AARO, which evaluates the UAP in April with 'moderate confidence' as a commercial helium balloon used for lighting. However, a senior-level aerospace engineer doubts the explanation and suspects that the UAP demonstrates some characteristics of plasma actuator technology. (Christopher Saul, 'Members of Congress Lambast Eglin General over UFO Information,' Niceville (Fla.) Mid Bay News, July 21, 2023; Christopher Sharp, 'U.S. Air Force Reports Mysterious Gulf of Mexico Incident to Pentagon's UFO Office,' Liberation Times, September 20, 2023; Keith Basterfield, 'What Do We Know about the Early 2023 Gulf of Mexico UAP Encounter?' Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena-Scientific Research, September 24, 2023; Rob Coppinger, 'Eglin and the UFOs,' Combat Aircraft Journal 24, no. 11 (November 2023): 84-87; John Greenewald, 'Air Force Releases Details about 2023 UAP Sighting First Brought to Light by Congressman Matt Gaetz,' The Black Vault, March 6, 2024; Joseph Trevithick, 'Air Force Pilot's Bizarre Encounter with Capsule-Like Craft off Florida Declassified,' The War Zone, March 6, 2024; '(U) Case: 'Eglin UAP,'' All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office, cleared April 18, 2024; Howard Altman, 'Eglin AFB Pilot Likely Saw a Lighting Balloon, Not a UFO Pentagon Concludes,' The War Zone, April 24, 2024; Kevin D. Randle, 'AARO Pilot's Sighting Report,' A Different Perspective, May 6, 2024; Micah Hanks, 'This Well-Known UFO Debunker Is Skeptical of the DoD's Recent Investigations into Aerial Mysteries: Here's Why,' The Debrief, May 7, 2024; Condorman, 'A Deep Dive into the Gulf of Mexico UFO Encounter,' Liberation Times, June 7, 2024)

- 2023, January 29 - 11:15 p.m. A Canadian North air crew from Fort McMurray watches two unidentified yellow lights maneuvering east of the airfield at Yellowknife, Northwest Territories. The lights then shift to 10 nautical miles northwest of the city. The air traffic controller can see nothing and has no radar targets. ('Canadian North Crew Reports 'Lights in Sky' over Yellowknife,' Cabin Radio, February 11, 2023; CADORS Report, no. 2023C0560, February 17, 2023)
- 2023, February - Early morning. Pilots in two different aircraft observe a fast-moving, elongated white object in an undisclosed location in the US. The incident report states that the pilot 'observed [the] unexplained phenomenon at my 2 o'clock at what appeared to be near my altitude or above. I am not sure the distance. At first I thought it to be a reflection in my windscreen, but after moving my head around and point of view, the object stayed steady.' 'The object appeared rectangular or oblong and milky white,' the pilot states. 'My First Officer (FO) returned from the forward [lavatory] and observed the object moving briskly southbound with me after I asked him 'what is that'. It moved at steady speed southbound and disappeared.' 'I regret not taking a video, but was taken by what I was seeing,' the pilot adds about the fleeting encounter. Within minutes, the pilot receives radio communication from a nearby ARTCC asking 'if anyone had seen the light or object.' 'Another flight captained by a friend also witnessed the Unidentified Aerial Phenomena (UAP), along with numerous other aircraft,' the pilot writes. 'I have never witnessed anything like it in almost 30 years of flying.' (Micah Hanks, 'Pilot UFO Sightings Collected by NASA Reveal Serious Safety Concerns over Objects in US Airspace,' The Debrief, August 29, 2023)
- 2023, February 1 -Eight or nine UAP are detected over the Arctic Circle, and NORAD fighter jets are scrambled in an unsuccessful attempt to intercept them. The objects maneuver away at high speed. (Ross Coulthart, 'UAP Spotted over Arctic Circle Days before Chinese Spy Balloon,' NewsNation, November 2, 2023)
- 2023, February 1 - 9:30 p.m. Clark Watson observes, from his hotel room in Wyong, New South Wales, a white, blinking light move in from the direction of the ocean. It is joined by a second light and both dart around in the sky and move over the town at a high speed. The lights come in over Tuggerah Lake at around 1,500 feet, then swoop as low as 200-300 feet and move directly above his hotel. He records their movements with a 3-minute mobile phone video, which shows several indistinct, flickering lights moving around in the sky. At one point,  the two lights maneuver around a third, solid, unmoving white light before heading back the way they had come. About one hour later, Watson sees a huge triangle with dim lights on its corners move in from over the ocean. It hovers at 400 feet. (''Giant Triangle': Aussie's 'Mind-Blowing' UFO Encounter,' news.com.au, September 1, 2024)
- 2023, February 2 - 12:28 p.m. A metallic, reflective, rotating sphere is observed above Pesaro, Marche, Italy, moving to the east at an estimated altitude of 3,900 feet. The Italian Air Force report says the object does not correspond to

any known flights or balloon launches. (Stato Maggiore dell'Aeronautica, Reparto Generale Sicurezza, Avvistamenti Oggetti Volanti non Identificati, Anno 2023 (2024), p. 2)

- 2023, February 4 - The US Air Force shoots down a Chinese-operated high-altitude balloon over US territorial waters off the coast of South Carolina on the orders of US President Joe Biden. Debris from the wreckage is recovered and sent to the FBI Laboratory in Quantico, Virginia, for analysis. The balloon was first spotted in US airspace above the Aleutian Islands on January 28. The American and Canadian militaries announce that the balloon is for surveillance, while the Chinese government maintains it is a civilian (mainly meteorological) airship that has been blown off course. The US says that the balloon is capable of geolocating electronic communications and carries intelligence surveillance equipment inconsistent with that of a weather balloon. It adds that similar Chinese spy balloons have flown over more than 40 nations. Analysts say that its flight path and structural characteristics are unlike those of a typical weather balloon. American officials later disclose that they have been tracking the Chinese balloon since it was launched from Hainan. Its original destinations were likely Guam and Hawaii, but prevailing winds blew it off course and across North America. The balloon is able to communicate with Beijing using a US internet service provider. (Wikipedia, '2023 Chinese Balloon Incident'; Joseph Trevithick, 'Our First Look at Chinese Spy Balloon Debris Being Recovered,' The Drive: The War Zone, February 7, 2023; Ron Drost, 'The Chinese Balloon and Reflections from a MUFON Field Investigator as an Eyewitness,' MUFON Journal, no. 660 (April 2023): 17; Debbie Ziegelmeyer, 'My Personal Encounter with the Chinese Spy Balloon,' MUFON Journal, no. 660 (April 2023): 18, 22; Courtney Kube and Carol E. Lee, 'U.S. Intelligence Officials Determined the Chinese Spy Balloon Used a U.S. Internet Provider to Communicate,' NBC News, December 28, 2023)

2023, February 6 - Chris Rutkowski releases the Ufology Research report on 768 Canadian UFO sightings of 2022, an increase of about 6% over 2021. He notes that one person in Quebec had reported nearly 40 separate sightings, which may account for the higher number. About 8% of the Canadian reports for 2022 are classified as unexplained, while 14% are explained. Another 44% match the description of a conventional object and therefore have a probable explanation, while 34% lack sufficient evidence. Around the same time, UFO Sverige releases a summary report on the 241 cases the Swedish group received in 2022. Most are IFOs (86) or Insufficient information (62). Only one report, from Onsala, Sweden, on April 2, 2022, could be considered genuinely unexplained, in which a couple observed two flying triangles with lights on the bottom, flying silently west, much faster than an aircraft. (Chris Rutkowski, 'The 2022 Canadian UFO Survey,' Ufology Research, February 6, 2023; Chris Rutkowski, ''Closer Eye to the Skies': Researcher Sees Uptick in Sightings of UFOs,' Canadian Press, February 16, 2023; UFO-Sverige, 'Rapportstatistik 2022')

2023, February 10 - Another high-altitude object is shot down in the vicinity of Deadhorse, Alaska, over the Beaufort Sea, by an F-22 Raptor fighter that takes off from Elmendorf Air Force Base. It uses n AIM-9X Sidewinder heatseeking missile to shoot it down. The Department of Defense says it is the size of a small car and flying northeast at approximately 40,000 feet, posing a risk to civilian flights. The pilots say the object looks metallic and breaks into pieces. They lose sight of the material as it falls into the clouds. Recovery efforts by the Alaska National Guard flying Chinooks and Black Hawks over the sea ice fail to turn up any debris. (Wikipedia, '2023 Alaska High-Altitude Object'; Howard Altman and Joseph Trevithick, 'F-22 Shoots Down 'Object' Flying High over Alaskan Waters (Updated),' The Drive: The War Zone, February 10, 2023; Katie Rogers, 'Inside the Hunt for U.F.O.s at the End of the World,' New York Times, February 20, 2023; Seth H. Feinstein, 'Sleight of Hand: An Analysis to Identify the Three Objects Shot Down in February,' MUFON Journal, no. 660 (April 2023): 10-12; 'Read 'Secret' Memo for Trudeau on Unidentified Object Shot Down over Yukon,' CTV News, September 5, 2023; Howard Altman and Tyler Rogoway, 'Secret Memo Raises More Questions about UFO Shootdowns over Alaska, Canada,' The Drive: The War Zone, September 6, 2023; Powell, Scientist, 115; Geoff Cruikshank, 'Deadhorse UFO Incident: NORAD Logs Reveal Insights into Potential Defense Systems,' Liberation Times, October 7, 2024)

2023, February 11 - On previous orders of Canadian Prime Minister Justin Trudeau, a 'small, cylindrical object' flying at 40,000 feet is shot down over the Yukon Territory for violating Canadian air space. Both Canadian and US aircraft are scrambled, and a US F-22 jet fighter makes the kill with an AIM-9X missile. (Wikipedia, '2023 Yukon High-Altitude Object'; Stetson Payne, 'F-22 Shoots Down Another Object, This Time over Canada (Updated),' The Drive: The War Zone, February 11, 2023; Howard Altman and Tyler Rogoway, 'Secret Memo Raises More Questions about UFO Shootdowns over Alaska, Canada,' The Drive: The War Zone, September 6, 2023; Powell, Scientist, 115; 'Image Released of Mysterious Object Shot Down over Yukon in 2023,' CTV News, September 24, 2024; Joseph Trevithick, 'First Look at Mystery Object Shot Down over Canada by F-22 Raptor Last Year,' The War Zone, September 24, 2024; Condorman, 'An Analysis of the Yukon UFO Incident,'

Liberation Times, October 12, 2024; Chrissy Newton, 'Canadian Lawmaker Calls for Increased Transparency after Images Surface of UAP Shot Down over Yukon Territory,' The Debrief, October 18, 2024)

- 2023, February 11 - R. Chase Cockrell, Linda Murphy, and Mark Rodeghier publish a study showing that there was no significant increase in the number of UFO reports in 2020, as compared to 2019. They suggest that the COVID-19 pandemic, during which many people were staying at home and experiencing more anxiety and stress, did not result in an increase of reported UFOs, especially when the number of sightings involving Starlink satellite launches were factored out. (R. Chase Cockrell, Linda Murphy, and Mark Rodeghier, 'Social Factors and UFO Reports: Was the SARS-CoV-2 Pandemic Associated with an Increase in UFO Reporting?' Journal of Scientific Exploration 36, no. 4 (2023): 641-656)
- 2023, February 12 - 4:00 a.m. At least three different commercial flights over Quebec report 'seeing very strange lights in the sky, high above the flight paths' that are 'moving in a rapid and irregular way.' A crew member on a cargo flight from Chicago to Luxembourg reports to air traffic controllers that 'It looks like it's more than one and sort of circling.' (CADORS Report, no. 2023Q0623, February 21, 2023)
- 2023, February 12 - The US Air Force and National Guard shoot down a third small object above Lake Huron within the maritime territory of Michigan. It is octagonal with strings hanging down from it and is first detected on February 11 flying across northern Montana, Wisconsin, and the Upper Peninsula of Michigan at 20,000 feet. Airspace is temporarily closed in the Lake Huron area where the object is shot down, falling into Canadian waters.
