- 2023, November 17-18 - The Sol Foundation hosts a symposium on UAP research and policy at Stanford University, sponsored by the Garry Nolan Laboratory (Department of Pathology). (Sol Foundation, 'Nolan Laboratory (Department of Pathology) and the Stanford School of Medicine Present: The Sol Foundation Initiative for UAP Research and Policy'; Sol Foundation YouTube channel; Micah Hanks, 'The Sol Foundation, Academia's New UAP Think Tank, Releases Videos of Lectures from Its Inaugural Symposium,' The Debrief, February 12, 2024)
- 2023, November 18 - 11:02 a.m. Omni Air International Flight 1169 en route from Honolulu, Hawaii, to Fort Worth, Texas, encounters 'several bright, erratic lights directly in front and above their flight path' as it approaches Los Angeles International Airport in California. Concerned about a possible collision, Flight 1169 requests 'deviations 20 degrees left to avoid the lights.' Once granted by Air Traffic Control, the plane shifts course and then continues on its way without further incident. The flight returns to its original course, noting that the lights are '80 miles off their right side' and approximately over the El Toro FAA radio station, east of Costa Mesa, California. The FAA entry emphasizes that it is 'not a UAS [unmanned aerial system] report.' (Dustin Slaughter, 'As Congress Introduces New UAP Reporting Legislation for Pilots, FAA Reports Continue to Detail Flight Safety Incidents Posed by UAP,' The UAP Register, February 10, 2024)
- 2023, November 18 - 7:00 p.m. A large, whitish ball of light is seen in southwestern France, northern Spain, Portugal, and Italy. It rises into the sky then disappears, leaving a dense halo of milky smoke. The official explanation arrives more than an hour later when the French Minister of the Armed Forces explains that the reports are due to the test launch of an M51.3 submarine-launched ballistic missile from the Bay of Biscay. Paolo Toselli notes that although the online media continue to report people attributing the display to UFOs and aliens, 'this isn't reflected in the visual first-hand testimonies.' (Paolo Toselli, 'About Missiles and UFOs,' UAP Check, December 22, 2023)
- 2023, November 19 - AARO again contacts whistleblower David Grusch, assuring him of its authorization to handle classified material and inviting him to discuss his claims, but he declines. (John Greenewald, 'FOIA Documents Reveal AARO's Authorized and Repeated Attempts to Engage with David Grusch,' The Black Vault, April 18, 2024)
- 2023, November 19 - 2:30 p.m. Flights are suspended for nearly 4 hours at the Bir Tikendrajit International Airport in Imphal, Manipur, India, after many people report an unidentified white object slowly moving southward then hovering at a high altitude above the facility. Airport manager Chipemmi Keishing says he doesn't know whether the object is a drone, a UFO, or something else. The object moves away to the west at 4:05 p.m., just before sunset. The Indian Air Force dispatches two Dassault Rafale fighters to investigate, but they find nothing.

('Unidentified Object Flies above Imphal Airport, Flight Operations Suspended for Four Hours,' The New Indian Express, November 19, 2023; 'Manipur Airport on High Alert after Unidentified Flying Object Spotted,' India TV, November 19, 2023; 'Indian Air Force Dispatches 2 Rafale Jets to Investigate UFO Sighting near Imphal Airport,' DD News, November 20, 2023; 'UFO near Imphal Airport Was Visible Till Sometime before Sunset on Nov 19, Then Disappeared,' Deccan Herald, December 14, 2023)

- 2023, November 19 - 5:15 p.m. A man driving along Neff Road in Harrisonburg, Virginia, sees a silent, tube-shaped object on November 19, 2023, that disappears before he can stop his car to take a photo. He resumes driving, and sees two more objects, one below the other, but again they disappear before he can pull over. (Jacob Fife, ''Did I Really See What I Thought I Did?' Harrisonburg Man Shares UFO Sighting,' WHSV-TV, Harrisonburg, Virginia, November 22, 2023)
- 2023, November 27 - The Dutch civilian group, UAP Coalition Netherlands, submits a proposal to the European Union Commission to extend the EU Space Law to include the search and detection of UAP through the EU Space Surveillance and Tracking Service and the Near-Earth Objects service. (Jochen Ickinger, 'UAP in the European Parliament, Part 1,' UAP Check, February 27, 2024)
- 2023, November 28 - Republican infighting threatens the bipartisan UAP Disclosure Act of 2023 as an amendment to the FY 2024 National Defense Authorization Act. Former Fox News host Tucker Carlson says on his show on X that 'Congressman Mike Rogers [R-Ala.], who is the chairman of the House Armed Services Committee, and Congressman Mike Turner [R-Ohio], who is chairman of the House Intel Committee....Both of these men have been instructed to violate, in letter and in spirit, federal law, and to hide the truth about UFOs.' Carlson, speaking to pro-disclosure Tim Burchett (R-Tenn.), also mentions that Speaker of the House Mike Johnson (R-La.) and Senate Minority Leader Mitch McConnell (R-Ky.) are also invested in shutting down the proposal. Lawyer Daniel Sheehan, who is president of the New Paradigm Institute, reveals in early December that the fifth lawmaker opposed to disclosure is Sen. Roger Wicker (R-Miss.), and all five are under pressure from private defense contractors and the intelligence community. Senate Majority Leader Chuck Schumer's (D-N.Y.) amendment has already been approved by the Senate, but is up for debate in the House. Meanwhile, the Pentagon provides a 33page, line by line rewrite of the Senate version to congressional negotiators that retains a modified provision for eminent domain over 'recovered technologies.' (Christopher Sharp, 'Black Friday: Republican Leadership Takes Axe to UFO Transparency Legislation,' Liberation Times, November 24, 2023; Micah Hanks, 'UAP Disclosure Act Receives Pushback from Lawmakers on Capitol Hill, As Bipartisan Fight for Transparency Continues,' The Debrief, November 27, 2023; 'Is Congress Suppressing UAP Disclosures? What to Know,' Newsweek, November 29, 2023; Christopher Sharp, 'Daniel Sheehan Exposes Five Powerful Republicans Blocking UFO Disclosure Act, As the Clock Ticks Down,' Liberation Times, December 3, 2023; Douglas Dean Johnson, 'The UAP Disclosure Act: The Proposed Pentagon/AARO Re-write of November 2023,' Mirador, July 24, 2024)
- 2023, November 28 - The tabloid Daily Mail (UK) reports that the CIA's Office of Global Access, established in 2003, is responsible for the retrieval of crashed UFOs. Three sources, all anonymous, tell the newspaper that the CIA sends military special forces teams to pick up the material, which is then handed over to private aerospace companies for examination and possible retroengineering. News Nation's Ross Coulthart thinks the report is credible, despite the lack of any direct evidence. (Josh Boswell, Chris Sharp, and Matt Ford, 'CIA's Secret Office Has Conducted UFO Retrieval Missions on at Least Nine Crash Sites around the World, Whistleblowers Reveal,' Daily Mail (UK), November 28, 2023; 'New Reporting on CIA's Role in UFO Retrieval Accurate: Coulthart,' NewsNation You Tube channel, November 29, 2023)
- 2023, November 28 - The National UFO Historical Records Center (NUFOHRC) in Rio Rancho, New Mexico, acquires the case files and archival records of the Aerial Phenomena Research Organization (APRO), a civilian group headed by Jim and Coral Lorenzen from 1952 to the 1980s. These records have for many years been in private hands and unavailable to UFO researchers. NUFOHRC Executive Director David Marler states that the APRO collection consists of 13 file cabinets and some 60 boxes. NUFOHRC has long-term plans to digitize these files, along with those of the Center for UFO Studies and the National Investigations Committee on Aerial Phenomena, which are already housed there. (Curtis Segarra, 'UFO Document and Research Collection Growing in New Mexico,' KRQE-TV, Albuquerque, N.Mex., December 1, 2023; Red Pill Junkie [Miguel Romero], 'The LongLost APRO Files Have Been Recovered,' Daily Grail, December 2023; 'The National UFO Historical Records Center Acquires Archives of Aerial Phenomena Research Organization,' Roswell (N.Mex.) Daily Record, December 17, 2023)
- 2023, November 30 - The University of Texas at El Paso receives a five-year, $5 million grant from the US Air Force Office of Scientific Research to develop a program to track Unresolved Resident Space Objects (space junk). ('Air Force Awards UTEP Grant to Safeguard Assets in Space,' University of Texas at El Paso Newsfeed, November 30, 2023)
- 2023, December 1 - Sean Kirkpatrick spends his last day in the AARO office, completing the compilation of the first volume of official interviews with UAP observers for the congressionally mandated Historical Review Report. Deputy Director Timothy A. Phillips begins serving as acting director. (Brandi Vincent, 'Pentagon's UAP Investigation Chief to Depart Dec. 1,' DefenseScoop, November 30, 2023)
- 2023, December 2 - 11:04 p.m. Air Traffic Control staff at Great Falls International Airport, Montana, spots more than five 'lit objects flying in a circular pattern,' according to an FAA report. 'Lights changed in intensity consistent with circling or holding pattern at different altitudes.' While some features of the sighting are consistent with a Starlink launch, additional details are not: 'Movement not always circular in pattern, but at times appeared to form up in formation or move to/from the area.' Security personnel at Malmstrom Air Force Base, located 20 miles east, also witness the objects. (Dustin Slaughter, 'As Congress Introduces New UAP Reporting Legislation for Pilots, FAA Reports Continue to Detail Flight Safety Incidents Posed by UAP,' The UAP Register, February 10, 2024)

2023,

December 3 - 4:07 a.m. A Société Air France Airbus is flying east near Toronto, Ontario, when the crew reports

an object with 6 or 7 lights ahead and above its position. (CADORS Report, no. 2023O3732, December 12, 2023)

- 2023, December 6 - A House-Senate conference committee strips many provisions included by the Senate in its UAP Disclosure Act (the Schumer-Rounds Amendment) to the National Defense Authorization Act, including the establishment of a nine-person independent review panel to select UAP records for public release, new whistleblower and witness protections, amnesty for defense contractors and others in possession of UAP-related material, and a provision for eminent domain of UAP material held by private individuals or companies. It also prohibits defense spending on any UAP programs unless the Secretary of Defense approves a specific AARO recommendation and provides Congress with the details. Some suspect that the CIA, Lockheed Martin, and other defense contractors have succeeded in pressuring key House and Senate members to withdraw their approval for disclosure, despite widespread bipartisan support. (John Greenewald, 'New UAP Language in the Compromised National Defense Authorization Act for Fiscal Year 2024,' The Black Vault, December 7, 2023; Christopher Sharp, 'Operation Kill Bill: How World-Changing UFO Language Was Killed and Sliced Up into Little Pieces,' Liberation Times, December 8, 2023)
- 2023, December 6 - 6:00 p.m. A swarm of unidentified drones is detected above Langley Air Force Base in Hampton, Virginia, in the first of multiple incursions through December 23. The number of UAS (uncrewed aerial systems) varies, and they range in both size and configuration. The drones fly in a pattern: one or two fixed-wing drones positioned more than 100 feet in the air and smaller quadcopters, the size of 20-pound commercial drones, often below and flying slower. Occasionally, they hover. They appear first to the north, traverse the base, then continue south beyond the reach of radar. They repeat the pattern and disappear, typically by midnight. A spokesperson says that 'None of the incursions appeared to exhibit hostile intent but anything flying in our restricted airspace can pose a threat to flight safety.' The situation prompts a serious response and causes significant reverberations around the US military. Plane spotters using online flight-tracking software notice unusual activity above Langley during the month, including what look like USAF F-16 fighters conducting combat air patrols supported by aerial refueling tankers over parts of southeastern Virginia around December 13 and one of NASA's WB-57F aircraft flying circular orbits above Langley on December 18. Sen. Tim Kaine (D-Va.) later remarks that 'how to do base protection against UAS threats domestically [in US residential areas] is not as developed as it is in theaters of war.' (Joseph Trevithick and Tyler Rogoway, 'Mysterious Drones Swarmed Langley AFB for Weeks,' The War Zone, March 15, 2024; Matt Laslo, 'Tim Kaine on If Gov't Know What Flew over Langley,' Ask a Pol, March 26, 2024; 'Langley AFB Event Video,' Reddit, March 2024; Marik Von Rennenkampff, 'Drones or UFOs? Alarming Incursions Demand Answers,' The Hill, April 21, 2024; Kyle Warfel and Christopher Sharp, 'Witness Statements Reveal Alarming 'Drone' Incursions over Langley Air Force Base As Dronebustrers Failed to Intercept Objects,' Liberation Times, September 14, 2024; Joseph Trevithick, 'Protective Nets to Shield F-22s Eyed for Airbase Swarmed by Mystery Drones,' The War Zone, October 9, 2024; 'Mystery Drones Swarmed a U.S. Military Base for 17 Days; the Pentagon Is Stumped,' Wall Street Journal, October 12, 2024; 'Unidentified Objects Swarmed Langley Air Force Base in December 2023: Enigma's Data and Analysis,' Enigma Labs blog, October 15, 2024; Howard Altman, 'Here's What NORAD's Commander Just Told us about the Langley AFB Drone Incursions,' The War Zone, October 29, 2024)
- 2023, December 11 - The Scientific Coalition for UAP Studies publishes a paper by Robert M. Powell, Larry Hancock, and other contributors, on UAP shapes and sizes, kinematic and EM effects, and the presence or absence of sounds, collected from 301 reports submitted between 1947 and 2016. (Robert M. Powell, Larry Hancock, Laiba Hasan, Sarah Little, Robinson Truong, and Tobi Kamoru, 'The Reported Shape, Size, Kinematics,
- 2023,

Electromagnetic Effects, and Presence of Sound of Unidentified Aerial Phenomena from Select Reports, 19472016,' Scientific Coalition for UAP Studies, December 11, 2023)

- December 13 - After being stripped of much of the expansive language incorporated in the UAP Disclosure Act amendment, the US Senate approves the final, unamendable version of H.R. 2670, the 2024 National Defense Authorization Act, by a vote of 87-13. The US House passes it the following day by a vote of 310-118. The bill directs the National Archives to collect government documents about 'unidentified anomalous phenomena, technologies of unknown origin, and nonhuman intelligence.' Any records not officially disclosed must be made public within 25 years of their creation, unless the president determines that they must remain classified for national security reasons. Sen. Chuck Schumer (D-N.Y.) complains that the US government 'has gathered a great deal of information about UAPs over many decades but has refused to share it with the American people. That is wrong, and additionally it breeds mistrust.' Sen. Mike Rounds (R-S.Dak.) adds, 'There is, we believe, information and data that has been collected by more than just the Department of Defense, but by other agencies of the federal government as well.' These drone incidents are not reported to the AARO office. ('Majority Leader Schumer and Republican Senator Mike Rounds Floor Colloquy on Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Provisions in the NDAA and Future Legislation on UAPs,' Senate Democrats, December 13, 2023; Douglas Dean Johnson, 'Quick Guide to UAP-Related Provisions in the Final FY 2024 National Defense Authorization Act,' Mirador, December 14, 2023; 'Congress Orders U.F.O. Records Released But Drops Bid for Broader Disclosure,' New York Times, December 14, 2023; Christopher Sharp, 'U.S. Senators Express Frustration over Weakened UFO Disclosure Language,' Liberation Times, December 14, 2023; J. P. Hague, 'A Dramatic Dilution of the Disclosure Process: A Temporary Shore Break Against the Waves of Change?' The Debrief, December 22, 2023; Christopher Sharp, 'U.S. Government's UFO Office Confirms It Has Not Received Information on 'Drone' Incursions over Lamgley Air Force Base, As New Details Emerge,' Liberation Times, May 11, 2024)
- 2023, December 13 - UK Secretary of State for Science, Innovation, and Technology Michelle Donelan replies to a question asked by MP Stephen Metcalfe in the House of Commons and says the UK government has no plans to hold hearings on UAP, 'but of course we work very closely with our allies on all these topics.' (Keith Basterfield, 'U.K. Parliament and UAP,' Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena-Scientific Research, April 1, 2024)
- 2023, December 14 - Former AARO Director Sean Kirkpatrick, along with Richard M. Medina and Simon C. Brewer from the University of Utah's Department of Geography, publish a paper in which they examine UFO reports gathered by the National UFO Reporting Center from 2001 to 2010 without assessing their value and find a greater concentration of reports per population in the western US, the far northeastern US, and the areas around Evansville, Indiana, and Washington, DC. They admit their study is based on a 'noisy, crowdsourced dataset.' (R. M. Medina, S. C. Brewer, and S. M. Kirkpatrick, 'An Environmental Analysis of Public UAP Sightings and Sky View Potential,' Scientific Reports, 13, 22213 (December 14, 2023)
- 2023, December 14 - Retired Rear Admiral Timothy Gallaudet states that he is convinced that David Grusch's allegations about a UAP retrieval program are true, based on his experience in the military and as acting director of the National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration, and that the Earth is being visited by non-human intelligences. (Ross Coulthart, 'Former Navy Rear Admiral Supports UFO Whistleblower Claims,' NewsNation, December 14, 2023)
- 2023, December 14 - The Enigma project reports on some of the characteristics of the 12,000 UAP sightings it has received from people logging reports on its website or its iOS app since August 2022. ('12,000 UAP Sightings and Counting,' Enigma, December 14, 2023)
- 2023, December 14 - 7:30 p.m. Jonathan Butler is stargazing on the west bank of the James River in Virginia when he notices a blinking red and orange light above Langley Air Force Base. A second follows it, then others follow. He begins to see four or five lights in  a row. Eventually he spots some 40 'car-sized' spherical lights, blinking reddish-orange, above Langley. They are traveling at the same height that a Cessna aircraft might fly. They are traveling in the same flight path, but some of them stop and hover when they are passing above the base. Langley responds by shining large searchlights at the objects. Soon a large 'saucer-shaped' object approaches at a lower altitude from the south, passes by Newport News, and circles near Surry Nuclear Power Plant. Its semi-circular base is glowing orange and it has three glowing lights on top and a white light on its edge that is blinking rapidly and erratically. Butler thinks it is observing the smaller lights. He takes at least four videos of the lights. (''Reality Check' with Ross Coulthart: New Video Shows UAPs Swarm US Military Base,' NewsNation YouTube channel, April 18, 2024; Christopher Sharp, 'U.S. Government's UFO Office Confirms It Has Not Received Information on 'Drone' Incursions over Lamgley Air Force Base, As New Details Emerge,' Liberation Times, May 11, 2024)
- 2023, December 18 - The National UFO Reporting Center adds an interactive map feature to its website that shows the locations of sightings in its database. Markers in green indicate UFO reports within the past year. ('New! UFO Sightings Map,' National UFO Reporting Center, December 18, 2023)
- 2023, December 19 - The US Space Force denies an FOIA request by The Black Vault that seeks information on 'Fastwalkers' and 'Slowwalkers,' objects believed to be NORAD's terms for objects entering or leaving the atmosphere at either high speed or slow speed. The Space Force claims the information is exempt from disclosure under for national defense or foreign policy reasons. (John Greenewald, 'Space Force Says Documents on 'Fastwalkers' and 'Slowwalkers' All Exempt from Disclosure,' The Black Vault, December 22, 2023)
- 2023, December 20 - 12:05 p.m. A private Piper Cherokee Arrow is flying to the southwest about 50 miles northwest of Frederick, Maryland, when the pilot sees a 30-foot triangular object passing in the opposite direction at about 6,500-6,800 feet. The point of the triangle is pointed downward. ('UFO Report: Pilot Reports 30 Feet Triangular Figure at 6500 Feet, Real ATC,' You Can See ATC YouTube channel, December 24, 2023; 'Pilot Reports Mysterious Triangle UFO Fly Past Him in Audio Recording,' Newsweek, January 9, 2024; Micah Hanks, 'Pilot's Bizarre Encounter with 30-Foot-Tall Triangular UFO Prompts Aviation Safety Concerns,' The Debrief, January 10, 2024)
- 2023, December 22 - President Joe Biden signs H.R. 2670, the 2024 National Defense Authorization Act, which establishes a collection point for all UFO documents within the US National Archives. ('Statement from President Joe Biden on H.R. 2670, National Defense Authorization Act for Fiscal Year 2024,' The White House, December 22, 2023)
- 2023, December 31 - The Centralen Erforschungs-Netz außergewöhnlicher Himmels-Phänomene (CENAP) in Germany reports that it has received 807 UFO sightings in 2023. ('UFO-Forschung: CENAP UFO-Meldestelle Statistik 2023 zeigt weiter hohes Niveau von Meldeeingängen,' CENAP, December 31, 2023; 'UFO-Forschung: CENAP veröffentlicht die tatsächlichen UFO Identifizierungen von 2023 in Deutschland,' CENAP, January 26, 2024; Hansjürgen Köhler, 'More Than 800 Reports Received by CENAP (Germany) in 2023,' UAP Check, February 22, 2024)

## 2024

- 2024, January - A group called UAP Sweden announces a European Crash Retrieval Initiative that aims to map, locate, and possibly visit credible UFO/UAP crash sites in Europe. ('European Crash Retrieval Initiative')
- 2024, January - The Centro de Identificatión Aerospacial of the Argentine Air Force presents an analysis of 82 UFO cases that were reported in 2023, explaining them all as having natural causes. (Rubén Lianza, Informe de Resolución de Casos Recibidos en 2023, Centro de Identificación Aerospacial, 2024)
- 2024, January 4 - In an apparent extension of the December drone incidents above Langley, an F-22 Raptor stealth fighter encounters a small drone above or near Joint Base Langley-Eustis near Hampton, Virginia, forcing it to deviate from its course to avoid a collision. (Kyle Warfel and Christopher Sharp, 'F-22 Near Miss with Unidentified Object Outside Langley AFB,' Liberation Times, October 24, 2024)
- 2024, January 5 - The US Office of the Director of National Intelligence denies The Black Vault's request for records related to UFO whistleblower David Grusch from May 2022 when he submitted a 'Disclosure of Urgent Concerns' to the Inspector General for the Intelligence Community. A subsequent appeal is also denied on February 21. (John Greenewald, 'FOIA Request for Access to UFO Whistleblower Documents Denied by Intelligence Agency,' The Black Vault, January 8, 2024; John Greenewald, 'The Black Vault's Appeal Rejected in Pursuit of UFO Whistleblower's Communications with ICIG,' The Black Vault, February 21, 2024)
- 2024, January 7 - Alain Juillet, former director of intelligence within the French General Directorate of External Security (DGSE), tells UAPCheck News in an interview: 'What is terrible about unidentified objects, flying or otherwise, is that these are systems against which our armies are incapable of responding. We are faced with something we do not control.' He thinks a key characteristic is their transmedium abilities-to become unidentified submerged objects with little transition. ('OVNI: Alain Juillet, ancien directeur du renseignement de la DGSE @OPENBOXTVfr,' Sentinel News YouTube channel, January 7, 2024; Baptiste Friscourt, 'Exclusive: Former DGSE Intelligence Chief Alain Juillet on UFOs,' UAPCheck News, January 10, 2024)
- 2024, January 8 - AARO provides additional information to whistleblower David Grusch to address his concerns about confidentiality and classification handling and offering a standing invitation for an interview. (John Greenewald, 'FOIA Documents Reveal AARO's Authorized and Repeated Attempts to Engage with David Grusch,' The Black Vault, April 18, 2024)
- 2024, January 9 - A new bill, the Safe Airspace for Americans Act, is introduced into the House of Representatives by Reps. Robert Garcia (D-Calif.) and Glenn Grothman (R-Wis.). It aims to encourage UAP reporting to the Federal Aviation Administration by pilots and other civilian aviation personnel and offers legal protection against repercussions. The FAA would also be required to collect data and conduct investigations into UAP reports it receives, after which the information is to be shared with the DoD's All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office (AARO). ('Safe Airspace for Americans Act,' US House, January 9, 2024; Micah Hanks, 'New Bipartisan Bill Aims to Strengthen UAP Reporting from Civilian Pilots and Aviation Personnel,' The Debrief, January 11, 2024)
- 2024, January 12 - Morning. Legislators on the House Oversight Committee receive a closed-door briefing on UAPs from Thomas Monheim, inspector general of the intelligence community. Many emerging from the meeting say they remain frustrated in their attempts to get more information on David Grusch's sensational claims. However, Rep. Robert Garcia (D-Calif.) says that 'it's reasonable to say that everyone that was in the room received probably new information.' Rep. Tim Burchett (R-Tenn.) says the meeting provided 'some validity to what's been said.' ('Lawmakers Investigating UAPs, or UFOs, Remain Frustrated after Closed-Door Briefing with Government Watchdog,' CBS News, January 12, 2024; 'U.F.O.s Remain a Mystery to Lawmakers after Classified Briefing,' New York Times, January 12, 2024; 'Burchett: Classified UFO Briefing 'Energized' Congress on Issue, More Hearings Expected,' The Hill, January 13, 2024)
- 2024, January 12 - Scientists from the Instituto de Medicina Legal y Ciencias Forenses del Ministerio Público in Lima, Peru, present the results of their analysis of alleged humanoid mummies confiscated at the Jorge Chávez International Airport in Lima in October 2023 when they were in the process of being shipped to Mexico. Forensic archaeologist Flavio Estrada Moreno demonstrates that the mummies seized were constructs made up of composite bones from birds and mammals and assembled with metal plates and dressed in miniature human clothes. ('Especialistas del Ministerio Público confirmaron que momias incautadas en el aeropuerto Jorge Chávez no son reales,' Government of Peru, Nota de prensa, January 12, 2024)
- 2024, January 15 - Frédéric Courtade succeeds Vincent Costes as head of the French UFO agency, GEIPAN (Groupe d'Étude et d'Informations sur les Phénomènes Aérospatiaux Nonidentifiés). A materials engineer, Courtade has had 20 years of technical and managerial experience with the French space agency CNES. ('Un nouveau responsable au GEIPAN à compter du 15 janvier 2024,' GEIPAN Actualités, February 12, 2024)
- 2024, January 19 - Sean Kirkpatrick claims that his 'Historical Record Report, Volume 1' is being prepared for delivery to Congress and the public, although it is unclear whether he is submitting it to the Defense Office of Prepublication and Security Review. He also says that allegations of a crash/retrieval cover-up that 'captured the attention of policymakers and the public, driving legislative battles and dominating the public narrative' are largely the product of former AATIP personnel, namely associates of Robert Bigelow. He also states that 'none of the conspiracy-minded whistleblowers' have come to AARO to provide their evidence. However, David Grusch says he was in email contact with AARO in October 2023, but was concerned about the way the office is handling classified compartmentalized information. (Sean Kirkpatrick, 'Here's What I Learned As the U.S. Government's UFO Hunter,' Scientific American, January 19, 2024; Christopher Sharp, 'Former UFO Office Director's Opinions Draw Scrutiny on Impartiality and Investigation Handling,' Liberation Times, January 22, 2024; Billy Cox, 'Yeah, Blame the Conspiracy Nuts,' Life in Jonestown, January 22, 2024)
- 2024, January 23 - In a podcast with journalist Peter Bergen, former AARO head Sean Kirkpatrick doubles down on unsubstantiated stories by a 'core group of people' who are trying to get the government 'involved in the business of investigating aliens.' (Peter L. Bergen, 'Is the Pentagon Really Hiding Crashed Alien Spaceships?' In the Room podcast, January 23, 2024; Richard Luscombe, 'Pentagon ex-UFO Chief Says Conspiracy Theorists in Government Drive Spending,' The Guardian (UK), January 27, 2024)
- 2024, January 24 - The Department of Defense Inspector General releases an unclassified summary of its August 15, 2023, report on the DoD's actions regarding UAP. It reveals that different components within the DoD have developed varied processes for the collection, analysis, and identification of UAP incidents. This lack of uniformity and integration poses potential risks to national security and military forces. The summary states, 'the DoD's lack of a comprehensive, coordinated approach to address UAP may pose a threat to military forces and national security.' ('Unclassified Summary of Report No. DODIG-2023-109, 'Evaluation of the DoD's Actions Regarding Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena,' August 15, 2023,' Department of Defense Office of Inspector General, January 24, 2024; John Greenewald, 'IG's Evaluation of the DoD's Actions Regarding Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Finally Released,' The Black Vault, January 25, 2024; Howard Altman, 'Watchdog Says Pentagon's UAP Response May Pose Threat to National Security,' The Drive: The War Zone, January 25, 2024)
- 2024, January 30 -Larry J. Hancock, Ian M. Porritt, and other researchers associated with the Scientific Coalition of UAP Studies publish a third paper on reported UFO activities from 1945 to 1975, this time focusing on nine specific characteristics, including interactive flight, interference with military weapons systems, intrusions at

military installations, and observed occupants. Their earlier two papers in 2023 focused on incidents in the context of atomic weapons development and deployment. (Larry J. Hancock, Ian M. Porritt, et al., 'UAP Activity Pattern Study 1945-1975, Military and Public Activities,' Scientific Coalition for UAP Studies, January 30, 2024)

- 2024, January 31 - Portuguese MEP Francisco Guerreiro poses a question in the European Parliament on why the preparatory work for an EU Space Law does not include a UAP reporting and monitoring system. ('UAP Monitoring and Reporting in the EU Space Law,' European Parliament, Parliamentary Question E-000318/2024, January 31, 2024; Jochen Ickinger, 'UAP in the European Parliament, Part 2,' UAP Check, February 28, 2024)
- 2024, February - The inaugural issue of Limina: The Journal of UAP Studies is released by the Society for UAP Studies. It includes a research article by Massimo Teodorani titled 'Investigating UAP Events Using Astronomical Techniques' that outlines the data on UAP that might be obtained through magnetometry, photometry, and spectroscopy. ( Limina: The Journal of UAP Studies 1, no. 1 (Spring/Summer 2024))
- 2024, February - The MUFON Computer Management System suffers an unexpected shutdown for three months, resulting in the organization's inability to log incoming UFO reports. The system is reinstated in May. (Kevin D. Randle, 'Rob Swiatek, EM Effects, and a UFO Photo,' A Different Perspective, September 13, 2024)
- 2024, February 5 - At a plenary session of the European Parliament held to discuss a new European Space Law, Portuguese MEP Francisco Guerreiro calls for a Europe-wide coordinated reporting and investigation procedure for UAP in European airspace. European Commissioner for the Internal Market, Thierry Breton, clarifies that UAP reports are handled by the European Union Aviation Safety Agency. (Baptiste Friscourt, 'MEP Francisco Guerreiro Calls for a UAP Space Law in the European Parliament,' Sentinel News Substack, February 8, 2024; Christopher Sharp, 'European Parliament Member Advocates for Unified Approach to Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena in New Space Law,' Liberation Times, February 18, 2024)
- 2024, February 6 - US government agencies have until the end of the current fiscal year to 'review, identify, and organize each UAP record in its custody for disclosure to the public and transmission to the National Archives,' according to a memo sent by Laurence Brewer, chief records officer for the US Government, and Chris Naylor, NARA's executive for research services. (Adam Mazmanian, 'New Legislation Mandates a Governmentwide Repository of Records Dealing with 'Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena,'' NextGov, February 6, 2024; National Archives, 'Records Related to Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena (UAPs) at the National Archives')
- 2024, February 7 - 3:40 a.m. Pilots of a Cargojet Airways aircraft report seeing a 'strange light' approaching them near Lac Laforge, northern Quebec. The light resolves into 3-4 lights, then fades away. (Chris Rutkowski, 'UFOs Reported through Transport Canada on March 18-19, 2024,' Ufology Research, March 19, 2024)
- 2024, February 8 - 8:49 p.m. Seven distinct objects are observed over Blainville, Quebec. They are flying east to west and have a 'bright full red color.' (Chris Rutkowski, 'UFOs Reported through Transport Canada on March 1819, 2024,' Ufology Research, March 19, 2024)
- 2024, February 9 - 12:23 a.m. The pilot of a Jazz Aviation Bombardier Challenger CL600 business jet flying above Black Rock Mountain, Alberta, notices three bright lights that 'grow dimmer.' More lights appear and disappear for the next few minutes. Air traffic control reports no air traffic in the vicinity. (Chris Rutkowski, 'UFOs Reported through Transport Canada on March 18-19, 2024,' Ufology Research, March 19, 2024)
- 2024, February 11 - 3:36 a.m. The pilot of an Air Canada airliner observes a formation of 5 lights near Thunder Bay, Ontario, and notifies Canadian Air Defence. (Chris Rutkowski, 'UFOs Reported through Transport Canada on March 18-19, 2024,' Ufology Research, March 19, 2024)
- 2024, February 12 - An American Airlines pilot reports a high-speed object with a flashing orange light while flying between Boston, Massachusetts, and Harrisburg. Pennsylvania. An audio recording posted on YouTube allegedly documents the conversation between the unidentified pilot and air traffic control. (Timothy Frudd, 'Audio: Pilot Spots UFO Mid-Flight: Report,' American Military News, February 28, 2024; 'Pilot Spots UFO While Flying from Boston: 'Bright Orangy Thing,'' Flight Simulator Fantasy - F.S.F. YouTube channel, February 16, 2024)
- 2024, February 12 - British UFO researcher Gary Heseltine launches a new website that encourages UK pilots to submit their UAP reports for analysis by a team of civilian investigators. ('Welcome to UK Pilots Reporting UAP'; 'UK Pilots Reporting UAP: Press Release,' UAP News Center, February 13, 2024)
- 2024, February 16 - 7:04 p.m. Two pilots flying an airliner at 36,000 feet near or off the coast of South Carolina watch a large, triangular object, 'roughly the size of a football field,' moving at about 4,000 feet below them. The object has three white lights at its corners and a central red light at its rear, which blinks out after 30 seconds. Neither onboard radar for the Traffic Collision Avoidance System detects it. After about 3 minutes, the object appears to emit a static discharge and vanishes, leaving the pilots to wonder whether it had accelerated or merely gone dark.

They capture a few photos of the object on a cellphone. (National UFO Reporting Center, 'NUFORC Sighting 180940,' April 27, 2024)

- 2024, February 21 - During an appropriations hearing at the state capitol in Harrisburg, David Padfield, director of the Pennsylvania Emergency Management Agency, admits that UAP have been reported in the vicinity of the state's nuclear power plants. The statement is in response to a question from State Rep. Ben Waxman (D-Philadelphia) about unmanned aerial systems at nuclear facilities. Padfield says that most UAPs re attributable to astronomical phenomena or military aircraft, but others are not easily explained. (Dustin Slaughter, ''Undefined' Sightings: Pa. State Emergency Management Director Acknowledges Receiving UAP Reports,' The UAP Register, February 22, 2024; Peter Hall, 'Pennsylvania Has Investigated More Than a Dozen UFO Incidents in the Past Decade, Records Show,' Pennsylvania Capital-Star, May 4, 2024)
- 2024, February 27 - 3:20 a.m. Two witnesses in Pozzuoli, Campania, Italy, see three whitish lights flying at a high altitude. After a few seconds, one manages to film the lights briefly before they disappear. ('UFO Sighting in Pozzuoli: Mysterious Lights in the Night Sky,' Il Mattino (Naples), April 5, 2024)
- 2024, February 27 or 28 - Night. A Mavic 3T thermal-imaging drone used by a unit of Ukraine's 406th Artillery Brigade to carry out frontline surveillance captures images of a silent, stationary, disc-shaped object. Based on the drone's altitude of 500 feet, the object could be about 30 miles away. ('Ukrainian Reconnaissance Drone Records Mystery Object in Warzone,' Kyiv Post, February 29, 2024; 'Filmte eine ukrainische Drohne ein UFO über dem Kriegsgebiet?' grenzWissenschaft-aktuelle.de, February 29, 2024)
- 2024, March - Retired Rear Admiral Timothy Gallaudet publishes a white paper through the SOL Foundation discussing the need for data and research on transmedium and underwater UAP and calling for a multi-agency, scientific, and international effort as a national ocean research priority. (Tim Gallaudet, Beneath the Surface: We May Learn More about UAP by Looking in the Ocean, SOL Foundation, White Paper 1, no. 1, March 2024)
- 2024, March 1 - Canada's Chief Science Advisor Mona Nemer announces that the first report of the Sky Canada Project will be ready in late summer or early fall. The project, launched in the fall of 2022, has contacted counterparts in the US and France, while also collecting information from Canadian departments and agencies like the Department of National Defence, Transport Canada, the Canadian Space Agency, and the Royal Canadian Mounted Police. (Canada, 'Sky Canada Project'; 'Canadian Government's Top Science Advisor Provides Update on Official UFO Study,' CTV News, March 1, 2024)
- 2024, March 1 - Ash Ellis's 'UK UFO Report 2023' shows that there were 395 sightings in the UK in 2023, a decrease of nearly 100 from 2022. The most commonly reported shapes are 'star-like' and 'orb,' making up 40% of the total. (Ash Ellis, 'UK UFO Report 2023,' March 1, 2024)
- 2024, March 4 - According to a survey from Ufology Research, 570 Canadians officially filed UFO sighting reports in 2023. Chris Rutkowski says that the number of reported sightings is significant. (Ufology Research, Results of the 2023 Canadian UFO Survey, March 4, 2024; ''Significant' Number of UFO Sightings Reported in Canada Last Year: Survey,' CTV News Winnipeg, March 4, 2024)
- 2024, March 6 - AARO Acting Director Timothy Phillips holds a briefing on the imminent release of the office's historical report on UAP. He also mentions that the Pentagon has issued portable UAP detection kits, called the Gremlin System, consisting of an array of sensors that fit inside a protective case, for testing at a 'very large range in Texas.' The kits enable personnel to capture real-time data and  respond rapidly to UAP incidents as they occur. AARO intends to deploy the system at militarily significant locations or near critical US infrastructure. The AARO team began working on the sensors in October 2023. Phillips adds that AARO is receiving 90-110 UAP reports a month from military sources. Despite repeatedly reiterating commitments to public transparency regarding AARO's findings, the media engagement was by invitation only, and Pentagon press officials limited attendance to invitees. (Brandi Vincent, 'DOD Developing 'Gremlin' Capability to Help Personnel Collect RealTime UAP Data,' DefenseScoop, March 8, 2024; Joseph Trevithick, 'DoD to Deploy 'Gremlin' Sensors to Collect Intel on Unidentified Flying Objects,' The War Zone, March 8, 2024; John Greenewald, 'Private Press Briefing Transcript with Acting AARO Director Tim Phillips on the Historical Record Report Volume 1 from March 6, 2024,' The Black Vault, March 11, 2024)
- 2024, March 8 - The Department of Defense's All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office releases part one of its longawaited, congressionally mandated historical review of government UAP investigations to the public. The report, which contains AARO's findings spanning from 1945 to October 31, 2023, concludes that AARO has found no evidence for claims that the US government and private companies have had access to extraterrestrial technology or that any UFO sighting represents an extraterrestrial craft. 'All investigative efforts, at all levels of classification, concluded that most sightings were ordinary objects and phenomena and the result of misidentification,' according to spokesman Maj. Gen. Patrick S. Ryder. A second part will include any findings

resulting from interviews and research completed from November 1, 2023, through April 2024. The report spurs the US Congressional UAP Caucus to sign a joint letter to House Speaker Mike Johnson (R-La.) and House Leader Hakeem Jeffries (D-N.Y.), urging the establishment of a Select Subcommittee devoted to investigating UAP. (US Department of Defense, All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office, Report on the Historical Record of U.S. Government Involvement with Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena (UAP), Volume I, February 2024; US Department of Defense, 'Statement by Pentagon Press Secretary Maj. Gen. Pat Ryder on the Historical Report on Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena, Volume 1,' March 8, 2024; Brandi Vincent, 'DOD Developing 'Gremlin' Capability to Help Personnel Collect Real-Time UAP Data,' DefenseScoop, March 8, 2024; Julian E. Barnes, 'Pentagon Review Finds No Evidence of Alien Cover-Up,' New York Times, March 8, 2024; 'UFOs in Storage? Findings about Alien Technology from Exhaustive Pentagon Review,' ABC News, March 8, 2024; Tom Rogan, 'Pentagon's UFO Agency Report Shows Analytic Bias,' Washington (D.C.) Examiner, March 8, 2024; Robert Powell, 'AARO Report: Flawed, Unresponsive, Clueless, and Knavish,' @rpowell2u Twitter post, March 9, 2024; Kevin D. Randle, 'Main Stream Media and the ARRO Report,' A Different Perspective, March 11, 2024; Kevin D. Randle, 'More Commentary on AARO,' A Different Perspective, March 11, 2024; Christopher Sharp, 'House Speaker Urged to Establish Congressional Committee for UFO Investigation Amid Doubt and Concerns over Pentagon Report,' Liberation Times, March 13, 2024; 'U.S. Rep. Eric Burlison Calls for Creation of a House Committee to Investigate UFOs,' Springfield (Mo.) News-Leader, March 13, 2024; Barry Greenwood, 'The 2024 AARO Report,' Center for UFO Studies, March 17, 2024; Christopher Mellon, 'The Pentagon's New UAP Report Is Seriously Flawed,' The Debrief, April 12, 2024)

- 2024, March 10 - Early morning. After receiving a report of a pulsating orange blob above Scotts Bottom Road east of Green River, Wyoming, MUFON State Director Richard W. Beckwith heads to an observation point with a video camera, a telephoto lens, and a tripod. The light sems to oscillate back and forth for about 40 minutes, and at one point a smaller white light seems to emerge from it, hover for a few minutes, then disappear. Beckwith captures the object and its movements on video. ('City Attorney for Rock Springs Captures Video of 'UFO' near Green River,' Cowboy State Daily (Lander, Wyo.), March 11, 2024)
- 2024, March 11 - Portuguese MEP Francisco Guerreiro makes a motion for a resolution to update the European Union Regulation on the reporting and analysis of civil aviation incidents to include a common methodology for reporting UAP sightings. ('Motion for a Resolution on Updating the EU Regulation on the Reporting, Analysis, and Follow-up of Occurrences in Civil Aviation to Include UAP Reporting,' March 11, 2024)
- 2024, March 11 - 10:30 p.m. An Aerolíneas Argentinas (AR1652) pilot sees four oddly moving lights at a high altitude as he is approaching Neuquén, Argentina. He asks the control tower at the Presidente Péron Airport if there are any registered flights in that area, but they have none. The operator gives him permission to change course, but the pilot declines, still puzzled by the lights. ('¿Un OVNI en Neuquén? La advertencia de un piloto de Aerolinéas Argentinas por luces extrañas en el cielo,' LM Neuquén, March 13, 2024)
- 2024, March 13 - 3:20 a.m. An observer in Idaho Falls, Idaho, notices a chevron-shaped, dark-gray object with a wide wingspan moving swiftly overhead from north to northeast in an arc, covering the field of view in about one second. The object has seven bright lights in a V-formation. (National UFO Reporting Center, 'NUFORC Sighting 180825,' April 27, 2024)
- 2024, March 14 - At a hearing before the Senate Armed Services Committee, US Air Force Gen. Gregory Guillot, head of US Northern Command and NORAD, acknowledges the incursions of drone swarms at Langley AFB in December 2023. He also says that the number of incursions by unmanned aircraft systems (drones) along the USMexico border amount to more than 1,000 every month. The activity is linked to drug and human traffickers who are using the technology to monitor their operations. Although he does not think that the incursions currently present a serious defense threat, he is working on ways that NORAD and Northcom can standardize some procedures for dealing with the situation. (Matthew Olay, 'NORAD Commander: Incursions by Unmanned Aircraft Systems on Southern Border Likely Exceed 1,000 a Month,' US Department of Defense News, March 14, 2024; Joseph Trevithick and Tyler Rogoway, 'Mysterious Drones Swarmed Langley AFB for Weeks,' The War Zone, March 15, 2024)
- 2024, March 16 - 7:04 p.m. German astrophotographer Sebastian Voltmer is taking videos of the Moon with a smartphone attached to a telescope on the Spicherer Höhen, a historical landmark on the French side of the border just south of Saarbrücken, Germany. He captures a swiftly moving bright object flitting across the lunar terminator. The high speed is improbable for a satellite or spacecraft, and although it could be a meteor, the International Meteor Organization does not show a match with a known observation. The telescope's focus range rules out an insect, and a high-flying bird is unlikely because the sun has already set. ('Strange Lunar Observation!' spacemovie YouTube channel, March 16, 2024; Andreas Müller, 'Bekannter Astrofotograf filmt unidentifiziertes Objekt vor dem Mond,' grenzWissenschaft-aktuell.de, March 19, 2024)
- 2024, March 20 - Member of the European Parliament Francisco Guerreiro hosts an event on the need for a scientific assessment and reporting of UAPs in the European Union. The event is attended by UFO researchers from several countries as well as a number of parliamentarians. (Robert Fleischer, 'UFO Researchers and Parliamentarians Gathered to Discuss the EU's UAP Policy,' Sentinel News Substack, March 22, 2024; Yoann Lamant, 'Ryan Graves, Former US Navy F-18 Pilot and ASA Executive Director, Speaks at the European Parliament,' Sentinel News Substack, March 29, 2024; Estelle Moutet, 'When Pilots Meet UAP,' Sentinel News Substack, April 15, 2024; Baptiste Friscourt, 'Aviation Safety and Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Discussed at the European Parliament,' The Debrief, June 8, 2024)
- 2024, March 21 - Numerous UFO disclosure advocates rally outside Sen. Chuck Schumer's (D-N.Y.) New York City office on Third Avenue to support his advocacy for the 2024 UAP Disclosure Act amendment. ('UFO Truthers Converge on Chuck Schumer's NYC Office to Celebrate Humankind's 'ET Moment,'' New York Post, March 21, 2024)
- 2024, March 25 - 2:30 p.m. Spirit Airlines NKS 3380 passenger Michelle Reyes spots a 'flying cylinder' from her window as her plane is heading into LaGuardia Airport in New York City. One other passenger also notices the object. She takes a video that she posts on her Facebook page and alerts the Federal Aviation Administration by email. The object appears to be flying very fast and is only on five frames of the video. MUFON investigator Thomas Wertman estimates the object is at 2,500 feet and relatively close to the aircraft. However, Mick West thinks the object is moving slowly and is consistent in appearance with a balloon. ('Caught on Camera: Possible UFO Reported over New York's LaGuardia Airport: Banfield,' NewsNation YouTube channel, April 25, 2024; 'UFO Spotted over New York City? Here's Is the Truth,' The Economic Times, April 25, 2024; Metabunk.org thread, 'Michelle Reyes, LaGuardia UFO from Plane Window,' April 2024; National UFO Reporting Center, 'NUFORC Sighting 180939,' April 27, 2024)
- 2024, March 26 - Dustin Slaughter of the UAP Register files a lawsuit against the US Air Force after the National Air and Space Intelligence Center failed for more than a year to respond to a FOIA request for high-quality military videos showing UAP. The lawsuit follows another case filed in 2023 against the National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration for its failure to turn over records related to UAP. (Dustin Slaughter, 'We're Suing the U.S. Air Force and National Air & Space Intelligence Center or UAP Videos,' The UAP Register, March 26, 2024)
- 2024, March 28 - 8:30 p.m. A witness living 10 miles south of Superior, Wisconsin, is walking out to her car when she hears a noise and looks to the south. She sees three lights in a triangular shape descending slowly at a low altitude across her property line. The configuration stops and hovers briefly before it moves away and disappears after about one minute. (Ken Hayes, 'Why I Believe This UFO Story from Sheila in Wisconsin,' KKCB-FM, Duluth, Minnesota, April 2, 2024)
- 2024, March 28 - Night. Several witnesses in Scottsdale, Arizona, watch and capture on video a blue light moving silently up and down for more than 30 minutes. One video shows it as a streak of blue light, with multiple light blue spots moving across it, that passes in front of a cloud. (Jake Carter, 'Residents of Arizona Record Video of 'Alien' Blue UFO,' anomalien.com, April 2, 2024; 'Unidentified Blue Object in the Night Sky Stuns Arizona Residents,' News18 (India), April 3, 2024)
- 2024, March 31 - Greg Edgreen, a retired Army lieutenant-colonel who ran the Pentagon investigation into Havana Syndrome cases, tells CBS-TV's 60 Minutes that he thinks US officials have been directly attacked by Russia. White House staff, CIA officers, FBI agents, and military officers and their families are among those who believe they have been wounded by a secret weapon firing a high-energy beam of microwaves or ultrasound in cases officially referred to as 'anomalous health incidents.' Each has worked at some point in their careers on Russian affairs. Christo Grozev, a journalist for The Insider, an investigative magazine by Russian exiles, suspects that Russian Intelligence unit 29155 is involved in the attacks, which apparently use a directed acoustic energy weapon. Mark Zaid, the attorney for one of the victims, claims he knows of classified information that contradicts the official assessment by the US intelligence community, which announced in 2023 that it is 'very unlikely' that a foreign adversary is responsible for the syndrome cases. (Scott Pelley, et al., 'Russian Nexus Revealed during 60 Minutes Havana Syndrome Investigation into Potential Attacks on U.S. Officials,' CBS News, March 31, 2024; Roman Dobrokhotov, Christo Grozev, and Michael Weiss, 'Unraveling Havana Syndrome,' The Insider, March 31, 2024; Cornelius Dieckmann, et al., 'Setzten russische Agenten Mikrowellenwaffen Gegen USDiplomaten ein?' Der Spiegel, April 1, 2024; Tim McMillan, 'Explosive Investigation Links Russia's Shadowy 'Unit 29155' to 'Havana Syndrome' Attacks on U.S. Officials Worldwide,' The Debrief, April 1, 2024; Katie Bo Lillis and Jen Christensen, ''There Is So Much Anger': Havana Syndrome Victims Frustrated CIA Isn't Blaming Russia for Symptoms,' CNN, May 1, 2024; Brian Karem, ''Active Suppression of Witnesses': CIA Lied about 'Havana Syndrome,' Whistleblower Documents Reveal,' Salon, September 16, 2024)
- 2024, April 4 - Mexican journalist and ufologist Jaime Maussan holds a press conference at the Sheraton Hotel in Lima, Peru, and presents new studies of the human-like mummies from the Nazca area in addition to a summary of the previous test results that allegedly show one of them is pregnant with a fetus. After officials from the Peruvian Ministry of Culture interrupt the event to secure any mummies present (they are not), a team of three US forensic experts, including John McDowell, professor emeritus of forensic dentistry at the University of Colorado, offers to conduct an 'unbiased examination of the bodies.' ('Conferencia en vivo: Seres Tridáctilos de Nasca: La prueba definitive,' Maussan TV YouTube channel, April 4, 2024; Andreas Müller, 'US-Forensik-Expertenteam will 'Wei ß e Nazca Mumien' unabhängig untersuchen,' grenzWissenschaft-aktuelle.de, April 6, 2024; 'Alien Fever Dreams Fuel Peruvian Grave Robbings,' Reuters, April 6, 2024; Joshua J. McDowell, 'Nazca Tridactyl Mummies,' McDowell Law Firm, April 2024)
- 2024, April 8 - 11:30 a.m. Two people are in Irwin, Pennsylvania, entering the exit ramp from US Highway 30 to travel on Interstate 76 when they notice a smooth, white, cylindrical object low above the 'business district.' It  is distinctly visible against a cloudy background and seems rounded on the front end and square on the back. As the object rises higher in the sky, it becomes a brighter white. The total viewing time is 5-10 seconds. (Stan Gordon, 'Two Daylight, Low Level UFO/UAP Sightings Reported above Populated Communities in Southwest Pennsylvania, April 2024,' Stan Gordon's UFO Anomalies Zone, April 15, 2024)
- 2024, April 8 - 7:45 p.m. A witness in Seagoville, Texas, sees a triangular-shaped object less than 100 feet above the ground near the intersection of West Simonds and Bowers Road. About 30-50 feet wide, the object has bright white lights at each of its corners. It hovers silently before accelerating slowly and disappearing into a dark cloud. (National UFO Reporting Center, 'Another Dramatic Triangle: 78 New Reports Posted,' May 6, 2024)
- 2024, April 9 - At a hearing before the Senate Armed Services Committee, Joint Chiefs of Staff Chairman Gen. Charles Q. Brown Jr. is questioned on 'incursions on military installations.' Sen. Mark Kelly (D-Ariz.) states: 'We see consistent incursions around sensitive government facilities.' (Marik Von Rennenkampff, 'Drones or UFOs? Alarming Incursions Demand Answers,' The Hill, April 21, 2024)
- 2024, April 9 - During a meeting of Japan's House Committee on Security, Rep. Yoshiharu Asakawa raises a question about whether the government is utilizing a UAP tracking platform similar to the Gremlin System used by the US Army. Defense Minister Minoru Kihara replies that he will propose developing such a data collection system. Asakawa asks whether the Japanese military is capable of intercepting UAP over sensitive areas, citing a report of objects near Prime Minister Fumio Kishida's residence and Yokosuka Air Force Base. Defense officials reply that they have that capability, but are unable to verify the report. (Baptiste Friscourt, 'Japanese Minister of Defense Says Responding to Unidentified Objects Is 'Essential,'' Sentinel News Substack, April 9, 2024)
- 2024, April 12 - 9:56 p.m. Three military witnesses in Navarre, Florida, see a dark, triangle-shaped object, slightly larger than a commercial Airbus, hovering silently at about 8,000 feet. It has 16 lights, some bright, some dimmer, that seem to twinkle like stars. It begins moving slowly out of sight westbound, apparently from the area of USAF Hurlburt Field in Okaloosa County, in about 35 seconds. (National UFO Reporting Center, 'NUFORC Sighting 181169,' April 27, 2024)
- 2024, April 14 - 6:35 p.m. A witness in the Oakland area of Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania, sees a bright, metallic-silver, cigar-shaped object moving silently and swiftly in a straight line over nearby houses. She estimates its length as about three times the size of a blimp and its altitude as only 200 feet. (Stan Gordon, 'Two Daylight, Low Level UFO/UAP Sightings Reported above Populated Communities in Southwest Pennsylvania, April 2024,' Stan Gordon's UFO Anomalies Zone, April 15, 2024)
- 2024, April 16 - The All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office (AARO) releases previously classified Department of Homeland Security documents outlining a 2011 project proposal, Kona Blue, to collect and examine data on concert with the Defense Intelligence Agency's Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications Program (AAWSAP). (John Greenewald, 'DoD Releases 'KONA BLUE' Documents,' The Black Vault, April 16, 2024)
- 2024, April 17 - A few members of the House UAP Caucus, an informal bipartisan group, attend a classified briefing Program Central Office, in a Sensitive Compartmented Information Facility (SCIF). (Prior to the event, a group calling itself the UAP Caucus, but which is apparently unaffiliated with the House UAP Caucus, releases a list of questions it would like to have answered.) Afterward, Rep Tim Burchett (R-Fla.) mentions to a reporter that 'There's no reason any of that stuff was in a SCIF, other than to get us not to talk about it.' ('Congressional Briefing Packet: AARO Briefing in Historical Review and New Leadership,' UAP Caucus, April 16, 2024; Joe Khalil, 'Lawmakers Seek Answers in Classified AARO Briefing,' NewsNation, April 17, 2024; Matt Laslo,
- 'advanced aerospace vehicles.' The government contractor would have been the Bigelow Aerospace company in with AARO Acting Director Timothy Phillips and Maj. Gen. David W. Abba, director of the DoD Special Access
- 'UAP Caucus Co-Chairs Luna & Burchett (& Ogles) Post-SCIF Briefing 'Nothing Burger,'' Ask a Pol, April 18, 2024)
- 2024, April 18 - Through a FOIA request, AARO releases documents, emails, and text messages relating to the agency's attempts to interview whistleblower David Grusch in 2023 and 2024. (John Greenewald, 'FOIA Documents Reveal AARO's Authorized and Repeated Attempts to Engage with David Grusch,' The Black Vault, April 18, 2024; Steph Whiteside, 'Whistleblower David Grusch Refused to Meet with Pentagon UFO Office,' NewsNation, May 3, 2024; D. Dean Johnson, 'More Zig-Zags by UFO Whistleblower David Grusch,' X post, May 4, 2024)
- 2024, April 22 - The UAP Coalition Netherlands meets with the Dutch Safety Board (Onderzoeksraad voor Veiligheid) in The Hague, Netherlands, and alerts them to issues of aviation safety related to UAP and the importance of research and analysis into these incidents. (Joachim Dekkers, 'Dutch Safety Board Accepts UAP Reports,' UAP Check, October 5, 2024)
- 2024, April 22 - 3:35 p.m. A witness southeast of Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania, notices a silver-white cylinder that appears to be moving north to south in a straight line at about 1,000-2,000 feet over the area of Irwin or Jeanette. He takes one photo of the object with his cellphone as it passes behind some trees. The object has no apparent wings or tail and appears rounded in the front. It disappears behind houses and trees after a few seconds. (Stan Gordon, 'A Third Low Level Wingless Cylindrical UFO/UAP Has Been Reported in Daylight during April 2024 in Southwest Pennsylvania,' Stan Gordon's UFO Anomalies Zone, April 29, 2024)
- 2024, May 8 - AARO releases a four-page paper explaining the effects of forced perspective and parallax on observers' location, providing important context for the public and analysts alike. (All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office, 'Effect of Forced Perspective and Parallax View on UAP Observations,' May 2024; John Greenewald, 'AARO Explores Forced Perspective and Parallax in New UAP Analysis Paper,' The Black Vault, May 8, 2024)
- 2024, UFO 'Lead,'' CTV News, May 15, 2024)
- descriptions of UAP. It offers insight into how these optical illusions can distort estimates of UAP size, speed, and May 15 - A CTVNews investigation turns up evidence of Transport Canada's lackluster cooperation with the US on sharing UAP information. (Daniel Otis, 'U.S. Intelligence Officials Wanted to Meet with Transport Canada's
- 2024, May 16 - Rep. Tim Burchett (R-Tenn.) introduces H.R. 8424, the UAP Transparency Act, that would require the US government to release all of its UFO-related files within 270 days of its effective date. (Tim Burchett, 'Rep. Burchett Introduces UAP Transparency Act,' May 17, 2024)
- 2024, May 16-17 - The National Science Foundation-funded ASTRO ACCEL initiative, dedicated to fostering interdisciplinary research and education in astronomy and related fields. holds a private workshop on 'Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena (UAP): A Dialogue on Science, Public Engagement, and Communication' in Virginia, led by Tim Spuck (on temporary leave from Associated Universities Inc.) and Gretchen Stahlman of Florida State University's School of Information. A diverse group of 26 participants attend, including physical and social scientists, communication and media experts, educators, aviation specialists, and government representatives. According to attendee Salman Hameed of Hampshire College, the workshop's primary task is to create a common language for discussion of UAPO and assess various types of detection equipment. Some AARO officials also participated. (Baptiste Friscourt, 'The National Science Foundation Organized a Private UAP Workshop with AARO,' Sentinel News, May 24, 2024; 'Innovative Workshop Explores Communication Strategies and Science Literacy through Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena (UAP),' Medium: ASTRO ACCEL, May 27, 2024)
- 2024, May 21 - Aerospace executive and former UAP Task Force member Col. Karl E. Nell gives a presentation at the SALT iConnections New York 2024 event, a group of global investors and financial advisers founded in 2009, in which he states unequivocally that non-human intelligence exists, it is interacting with humanity, and that unelected government officials are aware of this and reluctant to acknowledge the existence of retrieval and reverse-engineering programs. (Christopher Sharp, 'Senior Military Officer States There Is 'Zeo Doubt' NonHuman Intelligence Interacting with Humanity Exists and Warns of Catastrophic Disclosure,' Liberation Times, May 21, 2024)
- 2024, May 22 - The US Senate Select Committee on Intelligence holds a closed-door voting session on amendments to the draft 2025 Intelligence Authorization Act (S. 4443), including a ban on funding for non-reported Special Access Programs, a Government Accountability Office audit of AARO, and whistleblower provisions. (US Senate, Intelligence Authorization Act for Fiscal Year 2025, Committee Report 118-181, June 12, 2024; D. Dean Johnson, 'UFO Congressional News: Senate Intelligence Committee Issues Report on FY 2025 Intelligence Authorization Act,' X post, June 24, 2024)
- 2024, May 28 - Japanese lawmakers form a nonpartisan council of 16 members chaired by former Minister of Defense Yasukazu Hamada that will ask the government to set up an agency to investigate UAP sightings from the perspective of national security. Former Environment Minister Shinjirō Koizumi will serve as secretary-general. The group will hold its founding general meeting on June 6. (Japan UAP Study Team, 'UFO Parliamentary League Established,' X post, May 28, 2024; 'Japan Lawmakers to Create Group for Government Probes into UFOs,' Japan Times, May 28, 2024; 'In the Name of Security, Diet Group Planned to Study UFOs,' Asahi Shimbun, May 29, 2024; Keith Basterfield, 'The Japanese Government and UAP,' Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena-Scientific Research, June 1, 2024)
- 2024, June - The Major Cities Chiefs Association, the organization representing US police executives in large US cities, issued an information resource on UAP. (Major Cities Chiefs Association, 'Reference Guide: Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena (UAP),' June 2024)
- 2024, June 5 - 1:00 a.m. Michael Lehmann, along with about a dozen other workers at Red Rocks Amphitheater near Morrison, Colorado, notice a large object hovering silently a few hundred feet above a hogback ridge about half a mile to the north. It looks disc-shaped and metallic, and it appears to be several hundred feet long. It is black in color with three levels of lighted windows. After one worker shines a flashlight at the object, it tilts at an angle and moves 'belly-first' to the east, then begins fading away until it is invisible after 30 seconds. (National UFO Reporting Center, 'NUFORC UFO Sighting 1817786,' June 11, 2024; ''Red Rocks UFO' Vanished When Hit with Beam from Flashlight, Says Witness,' Denver (Colo.) Gazette, June 28, 2024)
- 2024, June 6-7 - The Interdisciplinary Research Center for Extraterrestrial Studies at the Julius-Maximilian-University of Würzburg, Germany, holds a workshop to explore methods to advance the scientific study of UAP and discuss the psychological, legal, ethical, life science, political, and sociological implications of the phenomenon. (Interdisciplinary Research Center for Extraterrestrial Studies, 'IFEX UAP Workshop 2024'; Keith Basterfield, 'The Latest Academic UAP Workshop: Held in Germany,' Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena-Scientific Research, June 8, 2024)
- 2024, June 11 - No UAP-related amendment will be voted on by the US House of Representatives during consideration of the FY 2025 National Defense Authorization Act (H.R. 8070) on the House floor. Four UAP-related amendments are submitted to the Rules Committee, but none of them receive Rules Committee approval for floor consideration. Three of the amendments are submitted by Rep. Robert Garcia (D-Calif.), and one by Rep. Tim Burchett (R-Tenn.). Neither of the sponsors appear at an open Rules Committee hearing on June 11 to ask that their amendments be made in order. The non-approved amendments include Garcia's amendment based on the Schumer-Rounds UAP Disclosure Act, a measure that passed the Senate in July 2023 but was subsequently gutted in conference in late 2023, mostly due to Pentagon opposition. Germaneness is also a problem with an amendment by Garcia and Rep. Glenn Grothman (R-Wis.) to require the FAA to establish a UAP-reporting system for civilian aviation personnel. This amendment is based on the Garcia-Grothman Safe Air Space for Americans Act, H.R. 6967. The other disapproved amendments are Burchett's short amendment instructing the President to release all records 'relating to' UAP (based on Burchett's UAP Transparency Act, H.R. 8424), and Garcia's amendment to make three obscure and probably superfluous changes to the statutes governing the Pentagon's All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office. (D. Dean Johnson, 'Congressional UFO Update,' X post, June 12, 2024)
- 2024, June 12 - The US House Permanent Select Committee on Intelligence unanimously approves its version of the Fiscal Year 2025 Intelligence Authorization Act (H.R. 8512), which contains no UAP provisions but does offer expanded whistleblower protections for intelligence community employees. (D. Dean Johnson, 'House Intelligence Committee Approves FY 2025 Intelligence Authorization Act,' X post, June 13, 2024)
- 2024, June 13 - Former US President Donald Trump asserts on Logan Paul's Impaulsive podcast that pilots have told him credible stories of 'strange things that they see flying around out there.' ('The Donald Trump Interview: Impaulsive Ep. 418,' Impaulsive YouTube channel, June 13, 2024; 'Trump 'Not a Believer' in Aliens from Space or UFOs But 'Knows' Illegal Aliens Exist,' Toronto (Ont.) Sun, June 14, 2024)
- 2024, June 14 - The US House of Representatives passes its version of the FY 2025 National Defense Authorization Act (H.R. 8070) with no UAP-specific language. (D. Dean Johnson, 'UFO Congressional News: Senate Intelligence Committee Issues Report on FY 2025 Intelligence Authorization Act,' X post, June 24, 2024)
- 2024, June 17 - The US House Appropriations Committee issues an official explanatory report on the Defense appropriations bill (Report no. 118-557), which makes a public declaration of support for the All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office and 'its mission to improve national security and public understanding of UFO data.' (D. Dean Johnson, 'House Appropriations Committee Expresses Support for AARO,' X post, June 18, 2024)
- 2024, June 18 - A bipartisan group of ten US senators, including Senate Intelligence Committee Chairman Mark Warner (D-Va.), sends Federal Chief Information Officer Clare Martorana a letter asking for an update on implementation of classification reforms enacted in December 2023. 'The government systematically overclassifies too much information at great cost to both public trust and national security,' the group writes. 'At the same time, the government often fails to protect the nation's most important secrets.' (D. Dean Johnson, 'Ten Senators Press Administration about Over-Classification,' X post, June 18, 2024)
- 2024, June 21 - 10:00 p.m. Two witnesses see an extremely bright white and blue beam of light that illuminates their yard in Richland, Texas, stopping directly over their house for less than a second before disappearing like a flashlight being turned off. The light is silent and causes both to feel radiant heat and develop dry mouths and soured taste buds. They feel as if time is standing still and repeatedly question what they have seen. The beam feels like a 'scan,' and their Bluetooth boombox and cellphone go silent. The next day, the son of one of the witnesses, who was camping nearby, confirms seeing a bright light, with one friend identifying it as a UFO. (National UFO Reporting Center, 'NUFOC UFO Sighting 182039,' June 27, 2024)
- 2024, June 24 - The National UFO Historical Records Center in Rio Rancho, New Mexico, signs a memorandum of agreement with the Rio Rancho School District to move its case files into two portable classrooms later in 2024 as part of an educational partnership. The facilities will be roughly six times as large as the current space, which will be accessible to students, parents, and the general public. (Rio Rancho Public Schools, 'Rio Rancho Public Schools and National UFO Historical Records Center Announce Groundbreaking Partnership,' June 25, 2024; Ollie Reed Jr., 'A Place to Call Home: UFO Records Center Plans Close Encounter with Rio Rancho Public Schools,' Albuquerque (N.Mex.) Journal, June 30, 2024)
- 2024, July 2 - 11:15 a.m. A man is driving south on US Highway 119 about one mile south of Smithfield, Pennsylvania, when his radio gets 'staticky' and his car stalls out. He notices a large, black, cylindrical object, perhaps 30 feet long and about 300 feet overhead. There is a pale-green haze around one end of the object. It hovers for a few seconds then moves off behind some trees where it alternately hovers and moves east for a few seconds about one mile away. He gets out of his car to take a few pictures with this cellphone, but they only show a dark object with no detail. Another motorist has pulled over after experiencing car trouble and seeing the object. The batteries on both cars appear to be dead, but they start up again after about 30 minutes. ('Two Vehicles Stall on Pennsylvania Highway As Cylindrical UFO Passes Low Overhead (July 2, 2024),' Stan Gordon's UFO Anomalies Zone, September 8, 2024)
- 2024, July 4 - The French agency GEIPAN issues a 'Note d'Enquête' describing UAP reports from commercial airline pilots it has investigated since the fall of 2022. The sightings involve moving bright points of light that appear briefly in a limited area of the sky. Observation periods last up to two hours. However, GEIPAN states that the sightings are in fact observations of Starlink satellites illuminated briefly by the sun. ('Un nouveau type d'observation récurrente de PAN: Les flashs de satellites Starlink (ou cluster flares),' Note d'Enquête, Groupe d'Études et d'Informations sur les Phénomènes Aérospatiaux Non identifies, July 4, 2024)
- 2024, July 9 - 9:45 p.m. A slow-moving, black triangular UFO with three lights on its bottom is sighted just above telephone poles and a tree line in Sarasota County, Florida, near the intersection of Proctor Road and Honore Avenue, moving back and forth at a slow speed of about 15 mph. The sighting causes a noticeable silence in the area, with no sounds from frogs or crickets, and the witness hears a slight whooshing noise when the object is directly overhead. When a small private plane flies nearby, the UFO quickly speeds away to the north. The witness tries to take a photo, but the image comes out blurry, while other photos taken before and after are clear, suggesting possible interference. (National UFO Reporting Center, 'NUFORC UFO Sighting 182235,' August 22, 2024)
- 2024, July 9-10 - A light display observed in Eastern Europe is attributed to the release of gases from the upper stage of the European Space Agency's Ariane 6 rocket, launched from the Guiana Space Centre. (Luis Ruiz Noguez, 'UAP over Poland, Czechia, Slovakia, and Russia?' UAP Check, July 30, 2024)
- 2024, July 11 - The All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office releases two reports detailing the analysis of a metallic alloy, allegedly recovered from a crashed UFO near Roswell, New Mexico, in 1947. The analysis, contracted to the Oak Ridge National Laboratory, supposedly exhibits extraordinary properties, including functioning as a terahertz waveguide and generating antigravity capabilities. ORNL's investigation involves a series of advanced tests, including isotopic ratio measurements, elemental composition analysis, and structural characterization using microscopy and spectrometry techniques. The findings indicate that isotopic composition of the magnesium and lead in the specimen falls within the expected values for terrestrial materials. The bismuth layers within the specimen were intermixed with lead, which precludes the possibility of it functioning as a waveguide. The report shows that the specimen's characteristics are consistent with mid-20th-century magnesium alloy research and

development projects, which often involved the use of zinc, lead, and bismuth additives for various purposes, including corrosion resistance. The banding and structural features observed in the specimen align with manufacturing techniques from that era, such as vapor deposition. The provenance of the specimen is problematic, as it was sent to radio host Art Bell in 1996 by the grandson of a military officer who claimed it was retrieved from a UFO that crashed between the San Mateo Mountains and the Sierra Blanca west of Roswell-so not the standard Corona crash. Journalist Linda Moulton Howe later reported on the results of an analysis at an unnamed university, according to which the approximately 5-centimeter fragment is said to consist of a magnesium-zinc alloy up to 200 millimeters thick, which is also alternately layered with layers of bismuth just one to four microns thick. Moulton Howe later referred to statements by leading US materials scientists (including those from MIT and Sandia National Laboratories) and again to military sources, all of whom allegedly puzzled over the possible origin, manufacturing methods, and application of such an alloy. Later, Stanford engineer Harold E. Puthoff, known for his investigations into numerous paranormal phenomena, not only attested to the layered structure of the fragment, but also to its exotic properties (including that of a so-called terahertz waveguide). The fragment attracted attention again in 2018, when the To The Stars Academy wanted to examine the material sample again as part of its ADAM project, using a cooperative agreement with the US Army Combat Capabilities Development Command. However, any investigation seems to be lost after the academy became more of an entertainment company in 2017. ('Synopsis: Analysis of a Metallic Specimen,' Oak Ridge National Laboratory, July 2024; 'Supplement to Oak Ridge National Laboratory's Analysis of a Metallic Specimen,' All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office, July 2024; John Greenewald, 'AARO Releases Findings on Suspected Extraterrestrial Alloy,' The Black Vault, July 11, 2024; Matthew Gault, 'Pentagon Publishes Report on Material from an Alleged Alien Aircraft,' Gizmodo, July 11, 2024; Andreas Müller, 'US-UFO-Behörde veröffentlicht Analyseergebnisse zu angebl. UFO-Fragment,' grenzWissenschaft-aktuell.de, July 12, 2024; Andreas Müller, 'The A.D.A.M. Research Project: Wissenschafter wollen UFO-Materialen untersuchen,' grenzWissenschaft-aktuell.de, August 23, 2018; Andreas Müller, 'To the Stars Academys ADAM-Projeckt testet erste angebliche UFO-Materialproben,' grenzWissenschaft-aktuell.de, September 30, 2018; Andreas Müller, 'TTSA: US-Army kooperiert mit Tom DeLonge's UFO-Forschungsorganisation,' grenzWissenschaft-aktuell.de, October 22, 2019; 'Ancient Aliens: Metallic Fragments Sent by ETs?! (Season 18),' History YouTube channel, August 6, 2022; Sigma 2 Technical Committee on UAP, '2024 UAP News Summary, April-June,' Sentinel News, August 31, 2024) 2024, July 11 - Sen. Mike Rounds (R-S.Dak.) introduces a version of the Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Disclosure Act, with Sen. Chuck Schumer (D-N.Y) as lead co-sponsor. The text is submitted as a possible floor amendment (SA 2610) to the FY 2025 National Defense Authorization Act (S, 4638). The text appears to largely track the Schumer-Rounds UAPDA that was incorporated into the FY24 NDAA that passed the Senate on July 27, 2023. The 2023 version was later gutted in conference negotiations with the House of Representatives, due mostly to opposition from the Pentagon. The new proposal retains provisions providing for creation of a presidentially appointed, Senate-confirmed Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Records Review Board-a temporary federal agency with broad powers, including subpoena and eminent domain authorities. It also retains definitions for such key operative terms as 'non-human intelligence,' 'technologies of unknown origin,' 'unidentified anomalous phenomena,' and 'legacy program.' Sen. Kirsten Gillibrand (D-N.Y.) signs on as a cosponsor on July 23. (Douglas Dean Johnson, 'Senators Rounds and Schumer Submit UAP Disclosure Act As Possible Amendment to NDAA,' Mirador, July 13, 2024; D. Dean Johnson, 'Update,' X feed, July 25, 2024) 2024, July 29 - 1:15 a.m. A witness in Spring, Texas, sees a large black triangular craft with red lights at each of its three points and a large white light in the center on its underside. The craft is approximately 60 feet away and moving slowly to the north at an elevation of 65 ° .
